# Digest 20261002-072943

Date: 2026-10-02. Backfill run requested by the user with **`since: 2026-01-01`**. Searches run: egfr-nsclc, nsclc-resistance-nextgen, lung-key-evidence, lung-top-journals, lung-nonpharm, preprints, approvals-access.

## Source errors and gaps

- **preprints (medRxiv and bioRxiv feeds): both FAILED** with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'feedparser'`. `pip install -r requirements.txt` cannot find `feedparser>=6.0` in this environment, which is the same failure as in runs 20261002-072121 and 20261002-072853. No preprints were retrieved. Not retried.
- **Truncated (backfill incomplete):** 4 of the 5 PubMed searches hit `max_results` = 500 and are flagged `truncated`. Only 500 of the matches in the 2026-01-01 to 2026-10-02 window were retrieved: egfr-nsclc (1987 matches), nsclc-resistance-nextgen (756), lung-key-evidence (2097) and lung-nonpharm (713). Records not retrieved were not recorded as seen. To complete the backfill, run in smaller `since:` chunks (e.g. month by month), as `searches.yaml` suggests.
- approvals-access: 5 web results kept across the 3 queries. 4 were already recorded by run 20261002-072121 (zidesamtinib and zongertinib FDA approvals; Imdylltra and Imfinzi CHMP opinions), so only 1 is new. The nyemetoder.no query returned no 2026-dated decision pages (0 kept). Web results come from a general search engine and are not exhaustive.
- ClinicalTrials.gov "results posted" items below carry no outcome numbers in the monitor record. Read the posted results on the registry before citing findings.

## High

### egfr-nsclc

- **Central Nervous System Activity of Lazertinib in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC With Prior EGFR TKI Exposure: A Pooled Analysis of KCSG-LU20-15 and LU21-01.** This pooled analysis of two Korean phase II trials (KCSG-LU20-15 and LU21-01) looked at lazertinib in 72 patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC and CNS involvement after prior EGFR-TKI, 62.5% with leptomeningeal metastases. Intracranial ORR was 50.0% (n=46 evaluable) with median intracranial PFS of 15.2 months, and median PFS and OS were 10.6 and 17.8 months; outcomes were worse with leptomeningeal disease. It adds CNS efficacy data for a third-generation TKI after TKI exposure, mostly in T790M-negative or unknown patients. Limitations: post hoc pooling of single-arm studies, small evaluable numbers and no comparator.  
  Kim DH, Hong MH, Jung HA et al., Thorac Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42670582/

- **Three-year adjuvant treatment of icotinib in stage II-IIIA EGFR-mutated lung adenocarcinoma (ICWIP): a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 3 trial.** ICWIP was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 3 trial of 3 years of adjuvant icotinib versus placebo after adjuvant chemotherapy in 135 patients with resected stage II-IIIA EGFR-mutant adenocarcinoma. IRC-assessed median DFS was 48.2 vs 20.0 months (HR 0.54, 95% CI 0.34-0.87), but OS was not different (HR 0.84, 95% CI 0.39-1.81; 5-year OS 88.6% vs 83.4%). Treatment-related AEs were 48.5% vs 23.2%. It adds adjuvant first-generation TKI data, but the sample is small, OS is not reached, and the comparison is placebo rather than osimertinib.  
  Liu YT, Tang L, Han XH et al., Signal Transduct Target Ther, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42660909/

- **Osimertinib plus anlotinib in untreated, EGFR-mutated, advanced non-small-cell lung cancer with concurrent alterations: a multicenter phase II study.** This multicentre single-arm phase II study gave first-line osimertinib plus anlotinib to 38 patients with advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC carrying TP53, PIK3CA or RB1 co-mutations. At a median follow-up of 25.6 months, median PFS was 29.0 months (95% CI 18.6-NR), ORR was 73.7%, and 18.4% had grade 3 TRAEs with no grade 4 or deaths. It targets a high-risk co-mutated subgroup in which intensified first-line therapy is being explored. Limitations: small sample, no randomized control, and PFS confidence interval still open.  
  Yang X, Tan Q, Chen S et al., Oncologist, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42658660/

- **Sutetinib for Patients with Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Harboring Uncommon EGFR Mutations: A Multicenter, Open-Label, Phase IIb Trial.** This multicentre, open-label, single-arm phase IIb trial (NCT05168566) tested the irreversible EGFR-TKI sutetinib 80 mg daily in 99 TKI-naive patients with advanced NSCLC carrying uncommon EGFR mutations (G719X, S768I, L861Q or compound). IRC-confirmed ORR was 70.8% (95% CI 60.7-79.7), median DoR 12.0 months and median PFS 13.7 months, with higher ORR in compound mutations (84.4% vs 64.1%). Grade 3 or worse TRAEs occurred in 42.4%, mainly diarrhoea (28.3%). It adds prospective data for a population with few trial options. Limitations: single-arm with no comparator such as afatinib or osimertinib, median follow-up 16.6 months, and OS immature.  
  Wu F, Zhang W, Zhao Y et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42637134/

- **Ivonescimab plus chemotherapy versus placebo plus chemotherapy in patients with advanced EGFR-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer after disease progression on EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor therapy (HARMONi): a multicentre, randomised, double-blind, phase 3 trial.** HARMONi is a randomised, double-blind phase 3 trial (n=438) of ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bispecific) or placebo plus carboplatin-pemetrexed in EGFR-mutated non-squamous NSCLC that progressed after a third-generation EGFR-TKI. Median PFS was 6.8 vs 4.4 months (HR 0.52, 95% CI 0.41-0.66), while median OS was 16.8 vs 14.0 months (HR 0.79, 95% CI 0.62-1.01), which did not reach statistical significance. Serious TRAEs were 28% vs 15%. It is a key post-osimertinib dataset for a bispecific. Limitations: the OS benefit is not statistically significant, the trial was industry-funded, and 70% of patients were Asian.  
  Le X, Passaro A, Zhao Y et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636833/

- **Andamertinib: First Approval.** This Drugs 'First Approval' profile reports that andamertinib (Beijing Avistone) received conditional approval in China on 30 April 2026 for adults with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with an EGFR exon 20 insertion who progressed on or did not tolerate platinum chemotherapy. It adds a new exon 20 insertion-directed EGFR-TKI to the regulatory landscape. The abstract gives no efficacy numbers. The approval is conditional, limited to China, and based on development data summarised in a company-informed drug profile.  
  Blair HA, Drugs, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42616343/

- **Therapeutic targeting of RAS-mediated resistance in oncogene-driven lung cancer.** Using tissue and liquid biopsies from 590 patients with oncogene-driven lung cancer progressing on targeted therapy, including 312 on first-line osimertinib, the authors found RAS alterations in 11.2% of osimertinib-resistant cases, enriched for KRAS G12D (n=10) and rarely G12C (n=1). In two patient-derived models with acquired KRAS G12D/G12R, osimertinib combined with the G12D inhibitor zoldonrasib or the pan-RAS inhibitor daraxonrasib was synergistic in vitro and in vivo. It identifies a resistance mechanism with a clear treatment hypothesis. Limitations: the combination data are preclinical, from only two models, and the cohort is retrospective.  
  Facchinetti F, Friboulet L, Liao L et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42612796/

- **Mefatinib versus gefitinib as a first-line treatment for EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer: a randomized, double-blind, multicenter phase III study.** This randomized, double-blind phase III trial in China (CTR20192297) assigned 336 patients with advanced non-squamous NSCLC with ex19del or L858R 2:1 to the second-generation EGFR-TKI mefatinib or gefitinib. IRC-assessed median PFS was 13.7 vs 9.7 months (HR 0.68, 95% CI 0.53-0.87); the benefit was limited to L858R (13.7 vs 8.3 months, HR 0.55), with no difference for ex19del, and 30-month OS rates were 60.2% vs 54.3%. Grade 3 or worse TRAEs were 45.7% vs 24.8%. It adds a new first-line agent with phase III data. Limitations: the comparator is a first-generation TKI rather than osimertinib, follow-up is short, OS is immature, and the trial was China-only.  
  Yu J, Xiong A, Wang Q et al., Signal Transduct Target Ther, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42586967/

- **Osimertinib With or Without Chemotherapy in Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With EGFR and Concurrent TP53 Mutations: A Randomized Clinical Trial.** This multicentre, open-label phase 3 RCT at 17 Chinese sites (NCT04695925) randomized 294 patients with treatment-naive advanced EGFR-mutant non-squamous NSCLC and concurrent TP53 mutations to first-line osimertinib plus pemetrexed-carboplatin or osimertinib alone. Median PFS was 34.0 vs 15.6 months (HR 0.44, 95% CI 0.32-0.60), consistent across subgroups including brain metastases and L858R, and grade 3 or worse TRAEs were more frequent with the combination. It supports biomarker-selected intensification in a high-risk group, in line with FLAURA2. Limitations: open-label with investigator-assessed PFS, OS immature (30.6% maturity), and China-only enrolment.  
  Zhou T, Zhou H, Gao F et al., JAMA, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42574006/

- **Impact of Genomic Subtype on Intracranial Outcomes in Treatment-naive EGFR mutant NSCLC with Osimertinib (IGnITE).** IGnITE retrospectively studied 470 patients from 11 institutions with EGFR-mutant NSCLC and brain metastases treated with first-line osimertinib with or without stereotactic radiosurgery (SRS). The 24-month cumulative incidence of CNS progression was 30% for exon 19 deletions, 47% for L858R and 76% for atypical mutations; compared with exon 19 deletions, the HR was 1.68 for L858R and 4.33 for atypical mutations. Upfront SRS was associated with fewer CNS progressions, with the largest benefit in atypical mutations. This addresses CNS efficacy by EGFR subtype, but the design is retrospective, so the SRS association may reflect selection bias.  
  Miao E, Wo E, Boe L et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42520966/

- **Assessing time to symptomatic progression, a patient-relevant efficacy endpoint, in the MARIPOSA study in non-small cell lung cancer.** This secondary-endpoint analysis of the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial (first-line amivantamab plus lazertinib vs osimertinib in EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC) assessed time to symptomatic progression (TTSP) at the final protocol-specified OS analysis (median follow-up 37.8 months). Median TTSP was 43.6 vs 29.3 months (HR 0.69, 95% CI 0.57-0.83; p<0.0001), and TTSP correlated strongly with PFS and OS. It adds a patient-relevant endpoint to the existing MARIPOSA PFS/OS benefit. Limitations: TTSP depends on clinician-driven intervention, the trial was open-label, and the death-censored analysis was exploratory.  
  Nguyen D, Lee SH, Lu S et al., Eur J Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42492306/

- **First-Line Sunvozertinib in NSCLC with EGFR Exon 20 Insertion Mutations.** WU-KONG28 phase 3 randomised 324 patients with advanced EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC to first-line sunvozertinib or carboplatin-pemetrexed. Median PFS was 10.3 vs 7.5 months (HR 0.65), ORR 58.9% vs 31.1%, median DoR 11.2 vs 7.1 months; grade ≥3 AEs 75.5% vs 56.7%. A new first-line option for exon 20 insertions with phase 3 data. Limitations: OS immature (38.9% maturity) with crossover allowed, higher toxicity, sponsor-funded.  
  Zhou C, Greillier L, Liu G et al., N Engl J Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42212913/

- **Compound EGFR Mutations Are Predominantly P-Loop and Alpha-C Helix Compressing Mutations With Increased Responsiveness to Second- Versus Third-Generation Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors.** Landscape study of 15,851 cfDNA-tested EGFR-mutant NSCLC samples with Ba/F3 drug-sensitivity testing and retrospective outcome data from 1,542 patients. PACC mutations (9% of samples) occurred mainly as in cis compound mutations (66%), and compound PACC mutations behaved like single PACC mutations, with greater sensitivity to second- than first- or third-generation TKIs, mirrored in clinical outcomes. Supports afatinib-type TKIs for compound PACC mutations. Limitations: retrospective, heterogeneous clinical cohorts, no prospective validation.  
  Liu X, Stamboulian M, Borgeaud M et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42191070/

- **Aumolertinib as adjuvant therapy in resected EGFR-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer (ARTS): a double-blind, multicentre, randomised, controlled, phase 3 trial.** ARTS is a double-blind phase 3 trial at 48 Chinese hospitals randomising 214 patients with completely resected stage II-IIIB EGFR ex19del/L858R NSCLC (95% after adjuvant chemotherapy) to aumolertinib 110 mg or placebo for 3 years. BICR-assessed DFS was significantly improved (HR 0.17, 95% CI 0.09-0.29), median not reached vs 19.4 months; grade 3-4 AEs were infrequent. Confirms the ADAURA-type adjuvant benefit with another third-generation TKI. Limitations: short follow-up (~27.6 months), OS immature, placebo comparator, China-only, sponsor-funded.  
  Zhang L, Zhang X, Wu L et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41539318/

#### New trial registrations

- **Osimertinib With or Without Primary Lung Tumor Resection for EGFR-Mutant Oligometastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer** (NCT07738172). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: National Taiwan University Hospital; interventions: Primary Tumor Resection, Osimertinib; enrollment 154; status RECRUITING. New registration: a multicentre, randomised, open-label phase III trial sponsored by National Taiwan University Hospital (planned enrollment 154, recruiting). Patients with EGFR ex19del/L858R oligometastatic NSCLC first receive osimertinib 80 mg for 12 weeks; those with partial response or stable disease who are fit for surgery are randomised 1:1 to continued osimertinib alone or primary lung tumour resection followed by resumed osimertinib. The primary aim is to test whether resection prolongs PFS; secondary endpoints include OS, safety, pathologic response, quality of life, progression patterns and molecular biomarkers. No results yet.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07738172

- **Pivotal Study to Evaluate YL202 Versus Docetaxel in Patients With Locally Advanced or Metastatic EGFR Sensitive Mutation Non-Squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer** (NCT07416994). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: MediLink Therapeutics (Suzhou) Co., Ltd.; interventions: YL202, Docetaxel; enrollment 440; status RECRUITING. New registration: a pivotal phase III trial sponsored by MediLink Therapeutics (planned enrollment 440, recruiting) comparing YL202 monotherapy with docetaxel. Participants have locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with an EGFR-sensitising mutation whose disease has failed both EGFR-TKI therapy and platinum-based chemotherapy. The primary aim is to show YL202 is more effective than docetaxel in this post-TKI, post-chemotherapy setting; the registry summary does not give endpoints or results.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07416994

- **Study of Izalontamab Brengitecan (BMS-986507) in Combination With Osimertinib Versus Osimertinib Monotherapy or Osimertinib in Combination With Platinum-based Chemotherapy for EGFRmt Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (IZABRIGHT-Lung02)** (NCT07680790). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: Bristol-Myers Squibb; interventions: Iza-bren, Osimertinib, Pemetrexed, Carboplatin, Cisplatin; enrollment 850; status NOT_YET_RECRUITING. New registration: IZABRIGHT-Lung02, a phase III trial sponsored by Bristol-Myers Squibb (planned enrollment 850, not yet recruiting). Previously untreated EGFR-mutant locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC is randomised to the EGFR-HER3 bispecific ADC izalontamab brengitecan (iza-bren, BMS-986507) plus osimertinib, osimertinib alone, or osimertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed chemotherapy (the FLAURA2 regimen). The aim is to test whether adding iza-bren improves outcomes over current first-line standards; endpoints are not detailed in the summary and no results are available.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07680790

- **A Study Comparing BL-B01D1 in Combination With Osimertinib Versus Osimertinib Alone in Patients With Locally Advanced, Unresectable EGFR-mutated Non-small Cell Lung Cancer(Stage III) Whose Disease Has Not Progressed Following Definitive Platinum-based Chemoradiation Therapy(PANKU-Lung08)** (NCT07640789). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd.; interventions: BL-B01D1, Osimertinib; enrollment 418; status RECRUITING. New registration: PANKU-Lung08, a registrational, randomised, open-label, multicentre phase III trial sponsored by Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical (planned enrollment 418, recruiting). Patients with unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC without progression after definitive platinum-based chemoradiotherapy receive the EGFR-HER3 bispecific ADC BL-B01D1 (iza-bren) plus osimertinib or osimertinib alone (the LAURA standard). The primary aim is to show better efficacy of the combination in this consolidation setting; specific endpoints are not given in the summary and no results are available.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07640789

- **Phase III Study of HS-10504 Versus Platinum-Based Doublet Chemotherapy in Patients With C797S+ NSCLC** (NCT07754123). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: Jiangsu Hansoh Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd.; interventions: HS-10504, platinum-based doublet chemotherapy (Pemetrexed and Cisplatin/Carboplatin); enrollment 206; status NOT_YET_RECRUITING. New registration: a randomised, controlled, open-label, multicentre phase III trial sponsored by Jiangsu Hansoh Pharmaceutical (planned enrollment 206, not yet recruiting). Patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC carrying an EGFR C797S mutation after failure of EGFR-TKI therapy receive oral HS-10504 monotherapy or pemetrexed plus cisplatin/carboplatin. The primary aim is to compare efficacy and safety against chemotherapy in this C797S resistance population; specific endpoints and results are not yet reported.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07754123

- **A Study of BL-B01D1 in Combination With Osimertinib as Perioperative Therapy in Patients With EGFR-mutated Resectable Non-small Cell Lung Cancer(PANKU-Lung09)** (NCT07642024). Phase: PHASE2, PHASE3; sponsor: Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd.; interventions: BL-B01D1, Osimertinib, Carboplatin, Pemetrexed, Cisplatin; enrollment 90; status RECRUITING. New registration: PANKU-Lung09, a registrational, randomised, open-label, multicentre phase II/III trial sponsored by Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical (listed enrollment 90, recruiting). Patients with resectable EGFR-mutant NSCLC receive perioperative BL-B01D1 (iza-bren, an EGFR-HER3 bispecific ADC) plus osimertinib, with osimertinib and platinum-pemetrexed listed as comparator interventions. The aim is to evaluate the efficacy and safety of the combination in the perioperative setting; the listed enrollment probably reflects only the phase II stage, and no endpoints or results are given in the summary.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07642024

- **A Phase Ⅲ Clinical Study of SYS6010 in Combination With Osimertinib in Patients With Locally Advanced or Metastatic NSCLC** (NCT07376382). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: CSPC Megalith Biopharmaceutical Co.,Ltd.; interventions: SYS6010, Osimertinib, platinum-pemetrexed; enrollment 680; status NOT_YET_RECRUITING. New registration: a randomised, open-label, multicentre phase III trial sponsored by CSPC Megalith Biopharmaceutical (planned enrollment about 680, not yet recruiting). Patients with EGFR-mutant locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC are randomised 1:1 to the EGFR-directed ADC SYS6010 plus osimertinib or investigator's choice of osimertinib or osimertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed. The aim is to test whether adding the ADC improves on current first-line standards; endpoints are not detailed in the summary and no results are available.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07376382

#### Results posted on ClinicalTrials.gov

- **A Study of Osimertinib With or Without Chemotherapy Versus Chemotherapy Alone as Neoadjuvant Therapy for Patients With EGFRm Positive Resectable Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer** (NCT04351555). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: AstraZeneca. Results were just posted on ClinicalTrials.gov for this AstraZeneca-sponsored, randomised, controlled, 3-arm, multicentre phase III trial (NCT04351555; enrollment 358, active not recruiting). It compares neoadjuvant osimertinib monotherapy, or osimertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed, with placebo plus chemotherapy in resectable EGFR-mutant NSCLC. The monitor record has no outcome numbers, so the posted results need to be read on the registry before drawing conclusions; mature survival data may not yet be available.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04351555

- **A Study to Evaluate Chemotherapy Plus Osimertinib Against Chemotherapy Plus Placebo in Patients With Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC)** (NCT04765059). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: AstraZeneca. Results were just posted for this AstraZeneca-sponsored randomised phase III trial (NCT04765059; enrollment 98, active not recruiting). It compares platinum-pemetrexed plus continued osimertinib with platinum-pemetrexed plus placebo in patients whose disease progressed extracranially on first-line osimertinib. This addresses whether to continue osimertinib beyond progression. The monitor record holds no outcome figures, and with 98 patients the trial is modest in size; the posted results should be checked on the registry.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04765059

- **ABC-lung: Atezolizumab, Bevacizumab and Chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant Non-small Cell Lung Carcinoma** (NCT04245085). Phase: PHASE2; sponsor: ETOP IBCSG Partners Foundation. Results were posted for ETOP 15-19 ABC-lung, an international, multicentre, open-label randomised phase II trial (ETOP IBCSG Partners Foundation; enrollment 95, completed). It has two non-comparative arms, atezolizumab-bevacizumab plus carboplatin-paclitaxel and atezolizumab-bevacizumab plus pemetrexed, in stage IIIB-IV non-squamous EGFR-mutant NSCLC after failure of EGFR-TKIs. The monitor record contains no outcome data; the arms are non-comparative and small, so any efficacy estimate is descriptive.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04245085

- **A Study of Lazertinib With Subcutaneous Amivantamab Compared With Intravenous Amivantamab in Participants With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor (EGFR)-Mutated Advanced or Metastatic Non-small Cell Lung Cancer** (NCT05388669). Phase: PHASE3; sponsor: Janssen Research & Development, LLC. Results were posted on ClinicalTrials.gov for this Janssen-sponsored phase III trial (NCT05388669; enrollment 418, active not recruiting). It compared subcutaneous amivantamab co-formulated with recombinant human hyaluronidase against intravenous amivantamab, both combined with lazertinib, in EGFR-mutant advanced or metastatic NSCLC, aiming to simplify administration and shorten dosing time. The monitor record has no outcome figures, so the posted data should be read on the registry.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT05388669

- **A Study Evaluating Platinum-Pemetrexed-Atezolizumab (+/-Bevacizumab) for Patients With Stage IIIB/IV Non-squamous Non-small Cell Lung Cancer With EGFR Mutations, ALK Rearrangement or ROS1 Fusion Progressing After Targeted Therapies** (NCT04042558). Phase: PHASE2; sponsor: Centre Francois Baclesse. Results were posted for this completed phase II trial sponsored by Centre Francois Baclesse (enrollment 150). It evaluated carboplatin/cisplatin-pemetrexed-atezolizumab, with bevacizumab when eligible, in stage IIIB/IV non-squamous NSCLC with EGFR mutations, ALK rearrangement or ROS1 fusion that progressed after targeted therapy. This is relevant to chemo-immunotherapy options after TKI failure. The monitor record holds no outcome figures, the population mixes several drivers, and the design is not randomised against a control.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04042558

- **Osimertinib Plus Savolitinib in EGFRm+/MET+ NSCLC Following Prior Osimertinib** (NCT03778229). Phase: PHASE2; sponsor: AstraZeneca. Results were posted for AstraZeneca's phase II SAVANNAH trial (NCT03778229; enrollment 367, active not recruiting). It studied osimertinib plus savolitinib (with a placebo-containing arm listed) in EGFR-mutant, MET-overexpressed/amplified locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC that progressed on osimertinib. It is central to MET-directed strategies after osimertinib. The monitor record contains no outcome numbers, so efficacy should be read from the registry posting; SAVANNAH is largely single-arm, so comparisons rely on its subgroups.  
  https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03778229

### nsclc-resistance-nextgen

- **SYS6010, epidermal growth factor receptor-targeting antibody-drug conjugate for advanced non-small cell lung cancer: A phase 1 trial.** This first-in-human phase 1 trial (ChiCTR2300072141) treated 236 patients with advanced NSCLC with SYS6010, an EGFR-targeting antibody-drug conjugate, and chose 4.2-4.8 mg/kg for expansion. ORR was 45.7% in EGFR-mutant NSCLC pretreated with EGFR-TKIs and 34.7% after both TKIs and platinum chemotherapy, where median PFS was 7.6 months and OS 19.4 months. Grade 3 or worse TRAEs occurred in 57.2%, mainly cytopenias. It is early clinical data for a new ADC in the post-TKI setting. Limitations: phase 1 with no comparator, a single-country population, and heterogeneous dose cohorts.  
  Li ZM, Zhou Z, He ZY et al., Cancer Cell, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42594871/

- **EGFR-mutant NSCLC progressing on first-line osimertinib: rebiopsy in real-world and impact of second-line therapies (the "Rebiopsy on Osi" study).** The multicentre retrospective 'Rebiopsy on Osi' study used the Italian ATLAS registry to examine 457 patients with common EGFR mutations whose disease progressed on first-line osimertinib. Rebiopsy was done in 45.1%, and a resistance mechanism was found in 38.8% of those (46.6% with tissue vs 13.5% with liquid biopsy); MET amplification/overexpression was the most frequent actionable finding. Biomarker-adapted second-line therapy (16.3% of treated patients, mostly MET-TKI regimens) was associated with median PFS 7.3 and OS 14.1 months, compared with 5.1 and 8.2 months without rebiopsy. These are real-world post-osimertinib data, but the design is retrospective, the adaptive group is small (n=39) and the comparisons are confounded by patient selection.  
  Gariazzo E, Colamartini F, Cantale O et al., Oncologist, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42502985/

- **Trastuzumab Deruxtecan in Patients From China With Pretreated HER2-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Final Analysis of the Phase 2, Single-Arm DESTINY-Lung05 Trial.** Final analysis of DESTINY-Lung05, an open-label single-arm multicentre phase 2 trial of trastuzumab deruxtecan 5.4 mg/kg every 3 weeks in 72 Chinese patients with pretreated HER2-mutant metastatic NSCLC. With a median follow-up of 20.2 months, confirmed ORR by independent review was 56.9% (95% CI 44.7-68.6), median PFS 9.9 months and median OS 21.0 months; grade >=3 drug-related AEs occurred in 55.6% and adjudicated ILD/pneumonitis in 12.5% (one grade 3). It supports a HER2-directed ADC in HER2-mutant NSCLC, as the criteria flag. Limitations: single-arm with no comparator, modest sample, Chinese patients only, and the population is primary HER2-mutant disease rather than post-EGFR-TKI resistance.  
  Liu Y, Li D, Wu L et al., Clin Lung Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42480250/

- **[Chinese Expert Consensus on Diagnosis and Treatment of Non-small Cell Lung Cancer with MET Abnormality (2026 Version)].** Updated (2026) Chinese expert consensus from the Lung Cancer Specialty Committee of the Chinese Elderly Health Care Association on diagnosis and treatment of NSCLC with MET abnormalities (exon 14 skipping, amplification, overexpression, fusions). Key changes from the 2025 version: a stronger recommendation for MET amplification and protein-overexpression testing in all newly diagnosed NSCLC and in patients with acquired EGFR-TKI resistance, and separate treatment protocols for MET amplification/overexpression after EGFR-TKI resistance versus driver-negative disease. It matters as practice guidance on MET-directed strategies after EGFR TKIs. Limitations: a regional expert consensus published in Chinese, and the abstract gives no specific drug recommendations or evidence grades.  
  Chen J, Han B, Hu Y et al., Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42452862/

- **Telisotuzumab Vedotin Monotherapy in Patients With Previously Treated c-Met Protein Overexpressing, Nonsquamous, EGFR Wild-type Advanced NSCLC: Updated Analysis of the LUMINOSITY Trial.** Updated analysis (about 6 months more follow-up) of the phase 2 LUMINOSITY trial of telisotuzumab vedotin 1.9 mg/kg every 2 weeks, a c-Met-directed MMAE ADC, in 172 previously treated patients with c-Met protein-overexpressing, EGFR wild-type, nonsquamous NSCLC (168 evaluable). ORR by central review was 29.2% overall (34.5% c-Met high, 23.8% c-Met intermediate) with median DOR 7.2 months, unaffected by prior platinum or ICI; peripheral sensory neuropathy was the main toxicity (31% any grade, 7% grade >=3). It is clinical data on a MET-directed ADC, now FDA-approved, though not in the post-EGFR-TKI population (EGFR-mutant patients were excluded). Limitations: single-arm, response-rate endpoint, and no mature PFS/OS in the abstract.  
  Girard N, Goldman J, Lu S et al., JTO Clin Res Rep, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42368479/

- **Histological transformation in lung cancer: a single-arm meta-analysis and systematic review.** Systematic review and single-arm meta-analysis of 35 prospective/retrospective studies and case series on histological transformation after EGFR-TKI resistance in EGFR-mutant lung cancer. Among TKI-resistant patients, the pooled proportion transforming to high-grade neuroendocrine carcinoma was 6% (95% CI 5-8%) and to non-neuroendocrine NSCLC 2%, at a pooled median of 19.4 months from TKI start; after transformation, pooled median PFS was 3.9 months and OS 10.7 months. It quantifies how often transformation occurs and how poor outcomes are, supporting re-biopsy at progression. Limitations: mostly retrospective, heterogeneous studies with variable re-biopsy rates and no comparison of post-transformation treatments.  
  Hu L, Zhu X, Chen X et al., BMC Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42163145/

- **Vebreltinib plus EGFR-TKI for EGFR-mutated NSCLC with MET-driven resistance: A real-world study of Chinese patients.** Single-centre retrospective Chinese study of 49 patients with EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC and MET amplification/overexpression (FISH, NGS or IHC 3+) after EGFR-TKI failure, treated with vebreltinib plus an EGFR-TKI. ORR was 46.9%, DCR 91.8%, median PFS 8.5 months and median OS 16.0 months; in 23 patients with brain metastases intracranial ORR was 69.6% and intracranial PFS 9.7 months, and MET IHC 3+ was associated with longer OS. Treatment-related AEs occurred in 67.3% (mostly grade 1-2, peripheral oedema 30.6%) with no permanent discontinuations. It adds clinical data on a MET-directed combination after EGFR-TKI resistance; limitations are the small, retrospective, single-arm design, mixed MET-selection methods and short follow-up.  
  Wang H, Cai Y, Jiang K et al., Lung Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42068890/

- **Long-Term Impact of First-Line Amivantamab Plus Lazertinib Versus Osimertinib on Mechanisms of Acquired Resistance in MARIPOSA: A Brief Report.** Brief report from the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial comparing acquired-resistance mechanisms (paired baseline/end-of-treatment ctDNA, Guardant360) with first-line amivantamab-lazertinib versus osimertinib in EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. MET amplification (3.4% vs 13.1%) and secondary EGFR mutations (1.4% vs 7.6%) were less frequent with amivantamab-lazertinib, without increases in other known pathways, and second-line PFS among patients starting subsequent therapy was longer (8.4 vs 5.3 months, HR 0.72). It suggests the combination changes resistance biology and informs post-progression treatment choice. Limitations: exploratory ctDNA analysis restricted to patients with evaluable samples, nominal p-values, and investigator-chosen, heterogeneous second-line therapies.  
  Hayashi H, Cho BC, Spigel DR et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42061572/

- **Efficacy, safety, and biomarker analysis of datopotamab deruxtecan in advanced non-small cell lung cancer: ICARUS-LUNG01 phase 2 study.** ICARUS-LUNG01 is a prospective phase 2 study of the TROP2 ADC datopotamab deruxtecan in 100 pretreated advanced NSCLC patients with longitudinal biomarker analyses. ORR was 26.0% and median PFS 3.6 months, with more benefit in non-squamous tumours; resistance was associated with absent cytoplasmic TROP2 staining and early DNA-repair activation, response with immune pathway activation. Relevant to ADC use after prior therapy. Limitations: single-arm, exploratory biomarkers needing phase 3 validation; EGFR-mutant results not reported in the abstract.  
  Planchard D, Cozic N, Mosele MF et al., Cancer Cell, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41999747/

- **Savolitinib plus osimertinib versus chemotherapy for advanced, EGFR mutation-positive, MET-amplified non-small-cell lung cancer in China (SACHI): interim analysis of a multicentre, open-label, phase 3 randomised controlled trial.** SACHI is an open-label phase 3 trial at 68 Chinese hospitals randomising 211 patients with EGFR-mutant, MET-amplified NSCLC progressing on EGFR TKIs to savolitinib plus osimertinib or platinum-pemetrexed. At interim analysis, median PFS was 9.8 vs 5.4 months in third-generation TKI-naive patients (HR 0.34) and 8.2 vs 4.5 months in the ITT population (HR 0.34); grade ≥3 AEs were 57% in both arms. First phase 3 evidence for a MET-directed combination after EGFR-TKI resistance. Limitations: open-label, investigator-assessed PFS, interim analysis, OS immature, Asian-only, and only 35% had prior third-generation TKI.  
  Lu S, Wang J, Yang N et al., Lancet, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41544643/

### lung-key-evidence

- **Perioperative Durvalumab for Resectable Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Updated Outcomes From the Phase III AEGEAN Trial.** This is an updated analysis of the phase III double-blind AEGEAN trial of perioperative durvalumab plus neoadjuvant chemotherapy versus chemotherapy plus placebo in resectable stage II-IIIB NSCLC (mITT n=740, EGFR/ALK excluded). At a median follow-up of 25.9 months, the EFS benefit persisted (HR 0.69, 95% CI 0.55-0.88), interim DFS favoured durvalumab (HR 0.66) and OS was numerically but not significantly improved (HR 0.89, 95% CI 0.70-1.14). It supports perioperative immunotherapy as a standard option. Limitations: OS is immature and interim, and EGFR/ALK-positive patients were excluded.  
  Heymach JV, He J, Harpole D et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42664473/

- **Consensus recommendations for the management of Dato-DXd-associated adverse events in patients with advanced/metastatic NSCLC: insights from a multiregional steering committee.** An international multidisciplinary steering committee used Delphi methodology to develop 55 consensus statements (all >75% agreement) on preventing, monitoring and managing datopotamab deruxtecan adverse events in advanced/metastatic NSCLC, focusing on oral mucositis/stomatitis, ILD/pneumonitis, ocular surface events, and nausea/vomiting. It matters as practical consensus guidance for an ADC used after EGFR-TKI progression. Limitations: expert opinion rather than new trial data, and the abstract does not report the specific recommended thresholds.  
  William WN, Brungs D, Fernandes de Sousa E Castro E et al., Ther Adv Med Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42558967/

- **Patient-reported outcomes with tarlatamab in extensive-stage small cell lung cancer after platinum-based chemotherapy: results from the phase 3 DeLLphi-304 trial.** Prespecified patient-reported-outcome analysis of the open-label phase 3 DeLLphi-304 trial (n=509) comparing tarlatamab (DLL3xCD3 bispecific T-cell engager) with standard chemotherapy in ES-SCLC after platinum-based first-line therapy. At 19 weeks more patients on tarlatamab reported improvement in chest pain (19% vs 10%), cough (35% vs 26%), dyspnoea (22% vs 7%), physical functioning (13% vs 8%) and global health status (23% vs 15%), with delayed time to deterioration and less bother from side effects. It complements the trial's previously reported efficacy with quality-of-life data supporting tarlatamab in second line. Limitations: open-label design with unblinded PRO assessment and questionnaire compliance falling to ~69% by week 19.  
  Mountzios G, Lammers PE, Sun L et al., Lung Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42679738/

- **Crizotinib versus observation or placebo for surgically resected early-stage ALK-positive non-small-cell lung cancer (Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group-American College of Radiology Imaging Network E4512): a phase 3 trial.** E4512 was a randomised phase 3 trial (ECOG-ACRIN) of adjuvant crizotinib for up to 2 years versus observation (initially placebo) in resected stage IB (>=4 cm)-IIIA ALK-positive NSCLC; accrual stopped at 166 of 168 planned when adjuvant alectinib was approved. In 153 centrally confirmed patients with median follow-up 65.7 months, DFS did not differ (median 74.6 vs 106.2 months, HR 1.08, 95% CI 0.61-1.90), and 58% on crizotinib had grade >=3 adverse events. It shows a first-generation ALK TKI gives no adjuvant DFS benefit, in contrast with alectinib in ALINA. Limitations: small, slow-accruing trial with design change from placebo to observation, wide confidence intervals and immature OS.  
  Gerber DE, Wang Y, Langer CJ et al., Lancet Respir Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42641639/

- **Adjuvant alectinib versus chemotherapy in resected ALK-positive non-small-cell lung cancer (ALINA): health-related quality-of-life and safety outcomes from a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial.** Safety and health-related quality-of-life report from the open-label phase 3 ALINA trial (n=257) of 24 months of adjuvant alectinib versus four cycles of platinum chemotherapy in resected stage IB-IIIA ALK-positive NSCLC. Alectinib's safety was consistent with its known profile (most common grade 3-4: CPK increase 6%), with fewer serious treatment-related AEs (2% vs 7%) and discontinuations (5% vs 13%) than chemotherapy; SF-36 scores showed clinically meaningful improvements by week 12 that were maintained over 2 years at population-norm levels. It supports adjuvant alectinib as standard of care alongside the previously reported DFS benefit. Limitations: open-label design, HRQoL an exploratory endpoint, and very different treatment durations between arms (24 months vs ~3.7 months).  
  Dziadziuszko R, Ahn JS, Barlesi F et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636834/

- **Daily low-dose carboplatin or weekly carboplatin plus nab-paclitaxel for concurrent chemoradiotherapy in older patients with locally advanced non-small cell lung cancer (JCOG1914): A randomized phase 3 trial.** JCOG1914 was an open-label phase 3 non-inferiority trial at 38 Japanese institutions in patients aged >=75 years with unresectable locally advanced NSCLC, comparing daily low-dose carboplatin with weekly carboplatin plus nab-paclitaxel, both with thoracic radiotherapy (durvalumab maintenance recommended). After 124 patients, the interim Bayesian predictive probability of non-inferiority was 8.0% and the trial stopped for futility (OS HR 1.56, 95% CI 0.79-3.11); the nab-paclitaxel arm had two treatment-related deaths and worse QoL at 6 weeks (OR 0.39). Daily low-dose carboplatin remains standard for older patients in Japan. Limitations: early termination with small numbers and immature OS, and Japan-only population.  
  Omori S, Kenmotsu H, Nomura S et al., Lung Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636548/

- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2026.3.3.** Latest version (2026.3.3) of the ASCO living guideline on systemic therapy for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, maintained by a standing expert panel with continuous literature review. The abstract contains only the standard living-guideline boilerplate, so the specific recommendation changes in this update must be checked in the full text. It matters because it sets current recommendations for EGFR and other driver-positive NSCLC. Limitation: no details of what changed are given in the abstract.  
  Wheatley-Price P, Ismaila N, Calles Blanco A et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42607282/

- **Guideline for the Comprehensive Perioperative Management of Older Patients With Lung Cancer (2025).** A Chinese guideline (2025) for comprehensive perioperative management of older patients with lung cancer, developed with GRADE and reported per RIGHT, covering 28 clinical questions (e.g. frailty, malnutrition, falls) with 55 recommendations. It emphasises comprehensive geriatric assessment, comorbidity management and early recognition of complications to reduce postoperative complications. Relevant as a new guideline for an under-studied population; the abstract gives no detail on evidence strength behind individual recommendations, and applicability outside its development setting is untested.  
  Li D, Liang R, Dong B et al., J Evid Based Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42576681/

- **Health Communication and Stepped Reminders Interventions for Lung Cancer Screening: A Randomized Clinical Trial.** Pragmatic 2x2 factorial RCT at Kaiser Permanente Washington (n=1837 with a normal prior LDCT) testing a health-communication intervention and a 'Stepped Reminders' intervention (pended orders for primary care plus patient outreach, coordinated via an EHR registry) to improve annual lung cancer screening adherence. Stepped Reminders increased adherence to 75.5% vs 47.4% (RR 1.59, 95% CI 1.47-1.72), whereas health communication slightly reduced it (59.2% vs 63.3%, RR 0.93). Relevant as a randomized screening-implementation trial with a large effect from a system-level intervention. Limitations: single integrated health system with a predominantly White population (88.7%), and the outcome is scan completion rather than stage or mortality.  
  Wernli KJ, Anderson ML, Palazzo L et al., JAMA Intern Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42574003/

- **ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Indolent Lung Cancer.** American College of Radiology Appropriateness Criteria on imaging surveillance of indolent lung cancer (persistent or slow-growing subsolid nodules and atypical lung cysts) when a multidisciplinary team chooses surveillance over intervention. Recommendations are developed by an expert panel using adapted GRADE and the RAND/UCLA appropriateness method. Relevant as a guideline update for managing indolent disease; the abstract does not give the specific recommendations, and where literature is lacking recommendations rest on expert opinion.  
  Expert Panel on Thoracic Imaging, Digumarthy SR, Messick B et al., J Am Coll Radiol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42573542/

- **SKYSCRAPER-03: a phase III study of tiragolumab plus atezolizumab versus durvalumab in locally advanced, unresectable, stage III NSCLC after platinum-based concurrent chemoradiation.** Open-label phase III SKYSCRAPER-03 randomized 829 patients with unresectable stage III NSCLC after concurrent chemoradiation to tiragolumab (anti-TIGIT) plus atezolizumab vs durvalumab consolidation. After median 33 months, IRF-assessed PFS in PD-L1-positive patients was 19.4 vs 16.6 months (HR 0.96, 95% CI 0.75-1.23, P=0.76), and OS was similar in all-comers (45.6 vs 45.8 months, HR 0.98); the primary endpoint was not met. A negative phase 3 result that further weakens the case for TIGIT blockade and keeps durvalumab as standard. Limitation: open-label design, and OS in the PD-L1-positive group is still immature.  
  Wakelee HA, Ahn MJ, Bradley J et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42562209/

- **Bridging evidence and practice: international multidisciplinary consensus on non-metastatic NSCLC.** International multidisciplinary modified-Delphi consensus (89 thoracic oncology experts, Barcelona, September 2025) on 96 statements about non-metastatic NSCLC; 62 reached consensus, 31 did not and 3 were rejected. Consensus supported routine FDG PET-CT staging, histologic confirmation of suspicious mediastinal nodes, reflex PD-L1 and DNA NGS at diagnosis, sublobar resection for selected node-negative tumours of 2 cm or less, and consolidation durvalumab regardless of PD-L1. Brain MRI in stage I, restaging after induction, RNA testing and ctDNA/MRD-guided perioperative decisions remained unresolved. Limitation: expert opinion, not new trial evidence.  
  Meyer ML, Peters S, Borghaei H et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42532338/

- **RATIONALE-303: Long-Term Outcomes of Tislelizumab in Previously Treated Advanced/Metastatic NSCLC.** Long-term (about 30 extra months) follow-up of the open-label phase 3 RATIONALE-303 trial, which randomized 805 patients with pretreated advanced NSCLC 2:1 to tislelizumab or docetaxel. Median OS was 16.9 vs 11.9 months (HR 0.67, 95% CI 0.57-0.80) in ITT and 19.3 vs 11.5 months (HR 0.52) with PD-L1 of 25% or more, with better PFS (4.2 vs 2.6 mo) and ORR (22.6% vs 7.8%) and fewer grade 3+ TRAEs (16.1% vs 66.3%). It confirms a durable OS benefit over docetaxel. Limitations: open-label design and a comparator that is now less relevant, since most patients today get immunotherapy first-line.  
  de Marchi P, Fan Y, Ma Z et al., JTO Clin Res Rep, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42529140/

- **Adjuvant Nivolumab vs Observation in Resected Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Randomized Clinical Trial.** This NCTN phase 3 trial (NCT02595944) randomised 935 patients with resected stage IB(≥4 cm)-IIIA EGFR/ALK-negative NSCLC, after standard adjuvant therapy, to 1 year of adjuvant nivolumab or observation. After 72.6 months median follow-up it was stopped for futility: median DFS 71.3 vs 68.8 months (HR 0.97), and 89.8 vs 78.5 months in PD-L1 ≥50% (HR 0.86, not significant). This is a negative result for adjuvant-only PD-1 blockade after upfront surgery, relevant to perioperative strategy choices. Open-label design; enrolment 2016-2019 predates current perioperative standards.  
  Chaft JE, Sun Z, Rudin CM et al., JAMA, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42224490/

- **Selpercatinib in Early-Stage RET Fusion-Positive Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.** LIBRETTO-432 randomised 151 patients with RET fusion-positive stage IB-IIIA NSCLC after definitive therapy to adjuvant selpercatinib or placebo for up to 3 years. In stage II-IIIA (n=109), 2-year investigator-assessed EFS was 92% vs 61% (HR 0.17, 95% CI 0.06-0.51); grade ≥3 ALT/AST increases occurred in 17%/19%. This establishes adjuvant targeted therapy in a second driver subgroup. Limitations: small trial, short follow-up (~2 years), OS immature, industry funded.  
  Wu YL, Hochmair M, Yang Y et al., N Engl J Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42223087/

- **Ivonescimab plus chemotherapy versus tislelizumab plus chemotherapy in advanced squamous non-small-cell lung cancer (HARMONi-6): interim overall survival analysis of a randomised, double-blind, phase 3 trial in China.** HARMONi-6 randomised 532 Chinese patients with untreated advanced squamous NSCLC to ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bispecific) or tislelizumab, each with paclitaxel-carboplatin. At the interim OS analysis (21.4 months median follow-up), median OS was 27.9 vs 23.7 months (HR 0.66, 95% CI 0.50-0.87), meeting the prespecified boundary; grade ≥3 treatment-related AEs were 69% vs 59%. First phase 3 OS benefit for a PD-1/VEGF bispecific over a PD-1 inhibitor. Limitations: China-only, 93% male, interim analysis triggered early (204 events) for regulatory deadlines, sponsor-funded.  
  Lu S, Liu B, Luo Y et al., Lancet, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42218899/

- **Lorlatinib versus crizotinib as first-line treatment for advanced ALK-positive non-small-cell lung cancer: 7-year update from the phase III CROWN study.** Post hoc 7-year update of phase 3 CROWN (n=296), first-line lorlatinib vs crizotinib in advanced ALK-positive NSCLC. Median PFS was not reached with lorlatinib vs 9.1 months (HR 0.19), 7-year PFS 55% vs 3%, and time to intracranial progression HR 0.06; no new intracranial progressions after 30 months. Supports durable first-line control including CNS. Limitations: post hoc, investigator-assessed, OS still immature.  
  Shaw AT, Solomon BJ, Felip E et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42217582/

### lung-top-journals

- **Izalontamab Brengitecan (Iza-Bren), a First-in-Class EGFR-HER3 Bispecific Antibody-Drug Conjugate in Extensive-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: Results From a Phase Ib Study.** This open-label multicentre phase Ib dose-expansion study (NCT05194982) gave the first-in-class EGFR-HER3 bispecific antibody-drug conjugate izalontamab brengitecan (iza-bren) 2.5 mg/kg to 52 patients with previously treated extensive-stage SCLC. ORR was 48.1%, median PFS 4.1 months and median OS 12.2 months; in second line (n=22), ORR was 72.7% and median PFS 6.2 months. Toxicity was mainly haematological, and HER3 expression may be associated with response. It is a new agent with activity in relapsed SCLC, but the study is small, single-arm and non-randomised; a phase III trial vs topotecan (NCT06500026) is ongoing.  
  Zhao Y, Zhao H, Wang Q et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42497381/

- **SHR-A2102, a nectin-4 directed antibody-drug conjugate, in patients with pretreated advanced solid tumours: a multicentre, single-arm, phase 1 trial.** Multicentre single-arm phase 1 trial (39 Chinese hospitals) of SHR-A2102, a nectin-4-directed ADC with a topoisomerase I inhibitor payload, dosed 2-10 mg/kg every 3 weeks in 395 pretreated patients, 197 of whom had NSCLC. Maximum tolerated dose was not reached (one DLT at 10 mg/kg); grade 3-4 treatment-related AEs occurred in 54% (mainly neutropenia 30%), serious TRAEs in 25%, and two treatment-related deaths; 6 and 8 mg/kg were taken forward. It is a new ADC class with a large NSCLC cohort, but the abstract gives no NSCLC-specific efficacy numbers, follow-up was short (median 7.2 months), the design is single-arm and all patients were Chinese.  
  Zhong R, Hong H, Yan M et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636836/

- **Targeting TROP2 in drug-tolerant persister cells delays EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor resistance in non-small-cell lung cancer.** Cancer Cell study showing that TROP2 is dynamically upregulated as EGFR-mutant NSCLC cells form drug-tolerant persisters under EGFR-TKI treatment: MAPK inhibition lowers c-Myc, which normally represses TROP2. Combining the TROP2 ADC sacituzumab tirumotecan with osimertinib suppressed persister emergence and delayed relapse in preclinical models, and the authors report preliminary efficacy from an ongoing first-line phase 2 trial of sac-TMT plus osimertinib. It gives a mechanistic rationale for front-line TROP2-ADC plus EGFR-TKI combinations aimed at preventing resistance. Limitations: the evidence is mainly preclinical, and the abstract gives no clinical numbers for the phase 2 trial.  
  Liao J, Yao W, Yu Y et al., Cancer Cell, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42314664/

- **SEZ6-targeting antibody-drug conjugate ABBV-706 in advanced small cell lung cancer and solid tumors: a phase 1 trial.** Open-label phase 1 trial (Nature Medicine) of ABBV-706, a SEZ6-directed ADC with a topoisomerase-1 inhibitor payload given every 3 weeks to 288 patients with advanced solid tumors, 240 on monotherapy including 124 with relapsed/refractory SCLC. In R/R SCLC the ORR was 52% (65/124); in dose optimization the ORR was 56% at 1.8 mg/kg and 59% at 2.5 mg/kg, and median OS at 1.8 mg/kg was 12.4 months, so 1.8 mg/kg was chosen as the RP2D. Grade >=3 TRAEs were common and dose-dependent (61% overall; 39% at 1.8 vs 70% at 2.5 mg/kg), mainly anemia. It is a promising new ADC for relapsed SCLC. Limitations: phase 1, non-randomized, with exploratory efficacy and survival endpoints.  
  Byers LA, Cho BC, Cooper AJ et al., Nat Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42225988/

- **Sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab versus pembrolizumab in PD-L1-positive advanced non-small-cell lung cancer (OptiTROP-Lung05): interim analysis of a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial.** OptiTROP-Lung05 is a randomised, open-label phase 3 trial at 68 Chinese hospitals comparing first-line sacituzumab tirumotecan (TROP2 ADC) plus pembrolizumab with pembrolizumab alone in 413 patients with PD-L1 TPS >=1% advanced NSCLC without targetable alterations. At the interim analysis (median follow-up 10.5 months), BICR-assessed median PFS was not reached versus 5.7 months (HR 0.35, 95% CI 0.26-0.47), with benefit in both TPS 1-49% (HR 0.28) and >=50% (HR 0.47). Grade >=3 adverse events were more frequent with the combination (55% vs 31%). Limitations: interim PFS-only analysis with short follow-up and no OS data yet, open-label design, China-only population, sponsor-funded; not in EGFR-mutant disease.  
  Xiong A, Yao W, Zheng W et al., Lancet, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42214392/

- **Thymic radiation is associated with worse outcomes in patients with NSCLC.** Multicohort analysis of 1,107 NSCLC patients treated with chemoradiotherapy (phase III RTOG-0617, n=460, plus two Harvard real-world cohorts with and without consolidation durvalumab) relating mean thymic radiation dose to outcomes, with a deep-learning proxy for thymic health. Each 1-Gy increase in thymic dose was associated with higher distant-metastasis risk (aHR 1.29 in RTOG-0617, 1.33 and 1.95 in the real-world cohorts), particularly in patients with preserved thymic function, alongside dose-dependent thymic decline and lower lymphocyte counts; thymus-sparing re-planning appeared feasible. It suggests the thymus may be an organ at risk worth sparing in thoracic radiotherapy. Limitations: observational, post hoc associations open to residual confounding, imaging-based proxy for thymic function, and no prospective test of thymic sparing.  
  Prudente V, Bernatz S, Pai S et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42641684/

- **Five-Year Outcomes of Perioperative Pembrolizumab for Early-Stage Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer From the Randomized KEYNOTE-671 Study.** Five-year update of the randomised phase 3 KEYNOTE-671 trial (n=797) of neoadjuvant pembrolizumab plus platinum chemotherapy followed by adjuvant pembrolizumab versus placebo in resectable stage II-IIIB (N2) NSCLC. With median follow-up of 60.4 months, 5-year EFS was 49.9% vs 26.5% (HR 0.58) and 5-year OS 64.6% vs 53.6% (HR 0.74), with no HRQoL detriment and no new safety signals. It confirms durable benefit supporting perioperative pembrolizumab as a standard of care. Limitations: the design cannot separate neoadjuvant from adjuvant contributions, and EFS was investigator-assessed.  
  Wakelee H, Spicer JD, Gao S et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42628840/

- **Biomarkers of nivolumab benefit in resectable non-small cell lung cancer.** Exploratory biomarker analysis of the phase 3 CheckMate 77T trial of perioperative nivolumab vs placebo in resectable NSCLC (Nature). Pre-surgical ctDNA clearance occurred in 66% of evaluable nivolumab patients (50% of whom had pCR) vs 38% with placebo (12% pCR); post-surgical MRD conversion during adjuvant therapy preceded recurrence in all cases, and EFS appeared longer with nivolumab in KEAP1/STK11/CDKN2A/SMARCA4-altered tumours (HR 0.48, 95% CI 0.28-0.83). It points to ctDNA clearance and co-mutation status as candidate predictors for perioperative immunotherapy. Limitations: only ~41% of randomized patients were biomarker-evaluable, analyses are post hoc/exploratory, and subgroups are small.  
  Cascone T, Awad MM, Spicer JD et al., Nature, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42587156/

- **Comprehensive Patient-Reported Outcomes from NRG Oncology/Alliance LU005: A Randomized Trial of Chemoradiation With or Without Atezolizumab in Limited-Stage SCLC.** Planned patient-reported outcome analysis of the randomized NRG-LU005/Alliance trial (n=544) of chemoradiation with or without atezolizumab in limited-stage SCLC, which previously showed no OS benefit from atezolizumab. Clinically meaningful decline in FACT-TOI at 15 months did not differ between arms (fewer declines with atezolizumab at 21 months), and EQ-5D-5L and fatigue scores were similar; twice-daily RT was associated with better FACT-TOI at all time points. It shows adding atezolizumab did not worsen quality of life and adds PRO support for twice-daily RT. Limitations: RT schedule was not randomized, and PRO compliance fell to 60-68% over follow-up.  
  Movsas B, Hu C, Higgins KA et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42532339/

- **SKYSCRAPER-01: Tiragolumab in Combination With Atezolizumab in Previously Untreated PD-L1-High, Locally Advanced, Unresectable or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.** Phase III SKYSCRAPER-01 randomized 521 patients with untreated PD-L1-high advanced NSCLC to tiragolumab (anti-TIGIT) plus atezolizumab or placebo plus atezolizumab. Investigator-assessed PFS was 7.0 vs 5.6 months (HR 0.78, 95% CI 0.63-0.97; not significant under the testing plan) and final OS was 23.1 vs 16.9 months (HR 0.87, 95% CI 0.7-1.1; P=0.22), with more grade 3-4 AEs (41.2% vs 33.8%). A definitive negative phase 3 result for anti-TIGIT in first-line PD-L1-high NSCLC. Limitation: the primary PFS analysis had short follow-up (median 9.9 months).  
  Peters S, Herbst R, Horinouchi H et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42507968/

- **Durvalumab With Radiation Therapy in Patients With Inoperable Locally Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Ineligible for Concurrent Chemoradiotherapy (DART).** Multicentre single-arm phase II DART trial of conventionally fractionated thoracic RT with concurrent and consolidative durvalumab (no chemotherapy) in 58 older or frail patients (median age 82) with inoperable stage III NSCLC who could not receive concurrent chemoradiotherapy. It met its primary endpoint with 2-year PFS of 39% (vs 20% historical) and 2-year OS of 54%; grade 3/4 TRAEs occurred in 21%, but 7% had grade 5 events and 31% stopped durvalumab early for AEs. Relevant for a population with no standard of care. Limitations: small, single-arm and compared with historical controls.  
  Rimner A, Lebow ES, Fitzgerald KJ et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42462186/

- **AdvanTIG-302: Phase 3 Study of Ociperlimab (Anti-TIGIT) Plus Tislelizumab (Anti-PD-1) Versus Pembrolizumab in Untreated, Locally Advanced, Unresectable, or Metastatic NSCLC With PD-L1 Expression of 50% or More.** Phase 3 AdvanTIG-302 randomized patients with untreated stage III/IV NSCLC with PD-L1 of 50% or more 5:5:2 to ociperlimab (anti-TIGIT) plus tislelizumab, pembrolizumab, or tislelizumab alone. The interim analysis (n=662) was stopped for futility: median OS was 31.9 vs 29.4 months for ociperlimab+tislelizumab vs pembrolizumab (HR 0.97, 95% CI 0.76-1.23), although PFS (14.3 vs 10.5 months) and ORR (61% vs 49%) were numerically higher. Another negative phase 3 TIGIT trial. Limitation: efficacy analyses after futility stopping are descriptive only.  
  Socinski MA, Reck M, Paz-Ares L et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42448175/

- **Phase III Study of Niraparib Plus Pembrolizumab as Maintenance Therapy for Advanced or Metastatic NSCLC (ZEAL-1L).** Randomized, double-blind phase 3 ZEAL-1L (n=666) tested niraparib plus pembrolizumab vs placebo plus pembrolizumab as maintenance after first-line chemo-pembrolizumab in advanced NSCLC without targetable drivers. In the primary population of responders (CR/PR, n=401), median PFS was 5.55 months in both arms (HR 1.00, 95% CI 0.79-1.27), so key secondary endpoints including OS were not formally tested; grade 3+ toxicity was mainly haematologic. A clearly negative phase 3 result for PARP inhibitor maintenance with immunotherapy. Limitation: OS remains untested.  
  Ramalingam SS, Velcheti V, Altan M et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42431263/

- **Consolidative Thoracic Radiotherapy With Atezolizumab Maintenance in Extensive-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Phase 2 TREASURE Randomized Clinical Trial (AIO-TRK-0320).** Open-label randomized phase 2 TREASURE trial (Germany/Austria) of consolidative thoracic RT (30 Gy/10 fractions) plus atezolizumab maintenance vs atezolizumab alone in ES-SCLC after carboplatin-etoposide-atezolizumab induction. Recruitment stopped early for safety after 68 patients: median OS was numerically shorter with TRT (6.7 vs 13.4 months, HR 1.55, 95% CI 0.90-2.69), PFS was similar (2.4 vs 2.6 months), and serious AEs (61.3% vs 18.2%) and fatal AEs (19.4% vs 3.0%) were higher, mainly infections and respiratory events. It argues against routine consolidative TRT with immunotherapy maintenance and runs counter to retrospective meta-analyses. Limitations: small sample, early termination, and a post hoc survival update.  
  Bozorgmehr F, Chung I, Behnisch R et al., JAMA Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42424047/

- **Ensartinib in Resected ALK-Positive Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.** Double-blind phase 3 ELEVATE trial (NEJM) randomized 274 patients with completely resected stage IB-IIIB ALK-positive NSCLC after adjuvant chemotherapy to ensartinib 225 mg daily or placebo for 24 months. Twenty-four-month DFS in stage II-IIIB was 86.4% vs 53.5% (HR 0.20, 95% CI 0.11-0.38), with similar results overall (HR 0.20); grade 3+ AEs occurred in 35.8% vs 18.2%. It adds a second ALK TKI with adjuvant phase 3 data after alectinib. Limitations: OS is immature, follow-up is short, and the trial was industry-funded and placebo- rather than active-controlled.  
  Yue D, Huang M, Song P et al., N Engl J Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42418775/

- **Ifebemtinib plus garsorasib as first-line treatment for KRASG12C-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer in China: a multicentre, single-arm expansion cohort from a phase 1b/2 trial.** Single-arm Simon two-stage phase 2 expansion cohort (8 sites in China, Lancet Respir Med) of first-line ifebemtinib (oral FAK inhibitor) plus garsorasib (KRAS G12C inhibitor) in 33 treatment-naive patients with KRAS G12C-mutated advanced NSCLC. Confirmed ORR was 82% (27/33; 95% CI 64.5-93.0) at median follow-up 21.5 months; grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 33% and serious AEs in 27%. Clinical data for a new combination that may address adaptive resistance to KRAS G12C inhibitors in the first-line setting. Limitations: small, single-arm, Chinese-only and 94% male; PFS/OS durability not shown in the abstract.  
  Song Z, Li X, Lin R et al., Lancet Respir Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42413526/

- **Differential impact of proton pump inhibitors and antibiotics on immunotherapy efficacy after chemoradiotherapy in locally advanced non-small-cell lung cancer: a post-hoc analysis of the PACIFIC trial.** Post hoc analysis of the phase 3 PACIFIC trial (660 of 713 patients, 5-year data) on baseline proton pump inhibitor and antibiotic exposure in unresectable stage III NSCLC treated with durvalumab or placebo after chemoradiotherapy. In the durvalumab arm, PPI exposure (40% of patients) was associated with shorter PFS (9.4 vs 17.2 mo, HR 1.57) and OS (33.0 vs 57.9 mo, HR 1.66); antibiotics were associated with shorter PFS only (HR 1.50). No such associations appeared with placebo, and treatment-by-PPI interaction was significant. It suggests concomitant medication review before consolidation immunotherapy. Limitations: post hoc and observational within the trial, exposure measured only at baseline, and residual confounding is likely.  
  Brunetti L, Santo V, Pinato DJ et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42398520/

- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2026.3.2.** ASCO living guideline update (version 2026.3.2) on systemic therapy for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, maintained by a standing expert panel with continuous literature review. The abstract does not list which recommendations changed in this version, so the full text is needed to see what is new (e.g. for EGFR-mutant disease). Relevant as the current ASCO reference for driver-positive NSCLC; a living guideline that is updated regularly.  
  Puri S, Ismaila N, Azar IH et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42372216/

- **Brief Report: Aumolertinib as a Switch Therapy in Osimertinib-Intolerant NSCLC-The ACTIVE Trial.** Prospective multicentre single-arm phase II ACTIVE trial (NCT04882345) in EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC patients who developed significant haematologic, liver or grade 3+ toxicity on osimertinib and were switched to aumolertinib 110 mg. Among 34 evaluable patients the 3-month conversion success rate was 70.6% (95% CI 52.5-84.9); median post-switch PFS was 11.6 months, 3-year OS 52.4%, and grade 3+ TRAEs 15.4% with no aumolertinib discontinuations. It supports switching between third-generation EGFR TKIs for toxicity rather than abandoning TKI therapy. Limitations: small brief report, single-arm, Chinese population, composite primary endpoint.  
  Li Z, Yao B, Huang C et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42341878/

- **Bispecific Antibody Ivonescimab Added to Chemotherapy in EGFR-Variant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The HARMONi-A Randomized Clinical Trial.** Final OS analysis of the randomized, double-blind phase 3 HARMONi-A trial (55 sites in China, n=322; JAMA) of ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bispecific) or placebo plus pemetrexed-carboplatin in EGFR-mutant nonsquamous NSCLC that progressed on EGFR-TKIs. At median follow-up 32.5 months, OS was 16.8 vs 14.1 months (HR 0.74, 95% CI 0.58-0.95; P=.02), with 30-month OS of 29.1% vs 18.4%; grade 3+ TEAEs were 67.1% vs 54.7%. A post-osimertinib-era option with an OS benefit, directly relevant to EGFR resistance. Limitations: Chinese-only population (not all had prior third-generation TKI), modest absolute gain of 2.7 months, and OS was a key secondary endpoint.  
  HARMONi-A Study Investigators, Fang W, Zhao Y et al., JAMA, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42307937/

- **Cemiplimab Plus Chemotherapy Versus Chemotherapy in Advanced NSCLC: 5-Year Results From Phase 3 EMPOWER-Lung 3 Part 2 Trial.** Five-year follow-up of the phase 3 EMPOWER-Lung 3 Part 2 trial, which randomized 466 patients with untreated advanced NSCLC without EGFR/ALK/ROS1 alterations 2:1 to cemiplimab plus chemotherapy or placebo plus chemotherapy. Median OS was 21.1 vs 12.9 months (HR 0.66, 95% CI 0.53-0.83), PFS 8.2 vs 5.5 months (HR 0.58), and 5-year OS 19.4% vs 8.8%; patients completing 36 cycles had 57.3% OS three years after stopping. Confirms durable benefit in both histologies. Limitations: chemotherapy-alone comparator, grade 3+ TEAEs 49.4% vs 32.7%, and late outcomes are descriptive.  
  Baramidze A, Makharadze T, Gogishvili M et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42297188/

- **Aumolertinib with or without chemotherapy in EGFR-mutated advanced non-small-cell lung cancer (AENEAS2): an open-label, multicentre, randomised, controlled, phase 3 trial.** Open-label randomized phase 3 AENEAS2 trial (60 hospitals in China, n=624) compared first-line aumolertinib plus pemetrexed-platinum vs aumolertinib alone in EGFR ex19del/L858R advanced NSCLC. BICR-assessed median PFS was 28.9 vs 18.9 months (HR 0.47, 95% CI 0.37-0.60) at median follow-up 23.4 months, with much more grade 3-4 haematologic toxicity (e.g. neutropenia 55% vs 1%) and serious AEs (36% vs 17%). It mirrors FLAURA2 with another third-generation TKI. Limitations: OS immature, open-label, Chinese-only population.  
  Li Z, Hu J, Chen J et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42296979/

- **IASLC Update on Classification of Pulmonary Neuroendocrine Neoplasms.** IASLC multidisciplinary expert-panel position paper updating the 2021 WHO classification of pulmonary neuroendocrine neoplasms. It proposes moving toward 'NET' nomenclature while keeping 'carcinoid' as the primary term, adding Ki-67 as a diagnostic criterion, and formally recognising 'carcinoid/NET G3' as a rare high-proliferation subset molecularly closer to NETs than to high-grade neuroendocrine carcinomas. Relevant as a consensus classification change that may affect diagnosis and treatment choice. Limitation: a proposal based on expert review, not yet validated prospectively or adopted by WHO.  
  Beasley MB, Yatabe Y, Papotti M et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42251892/

- **First-Line Serplulimab in Extensive-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: Secondary Analysis of the ASTRUM-005 Phase 3 Randomized Clinical Trial.** Prespecified secondary analysis with extended follow-up (median 42.4 months) of the double-blind phase 3 ASTRUM-005 trial, which randomized 585 patients with untreated ES-SCLC 2:1 to serplulimab or placebo with carboplatin-etoposide. Median OS was 15.8 vs 11.1 months (HR 0.60, 95% CI 0.49-0.73) and 4-year OS 21.9% vs 7.2%; grade 3+ treatment-related TEAEs were 35.0% vs 29.1%, and PROs were similar or favoured serplulimab. Confirms durable long-term benefit of a PD-1 inhibitor in ES-SCLC. Limitation: enrolment was mainly outside Western Europe/North America, and the platinum comparator was carboplatin-etoposide only.  
  Liu J, Han L, Wu L et al., JAMA Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42240984/

- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, 2026.3.1.** ASCO living guideline update (version 2026.3.1) on systemic therapy for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, including EGFR. The abstract only describes the living-guideline process and does not list the specific new recommendations, which must be read in the full text. Relevant as a guideline update for EGFR-mutant and other driver-positive NSCLC.  
  Reuss JE, Ismaila N, Ahluwalia A et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42190143/

- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Without Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, 2026.3.1.** ASCO living guideline update (version 2026.3.1) on systemic therapy for stage IV NSCLC without driver alterations. The abstract only describes the living-guideline process; the specific changed recommendations are not given and require the full text.  
  Bazhenova L, Ismaila N, Durm G et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42190141/

- **Tiragolumab Plus Atezolizumab and Chemotherapy for Advanced Nonsquamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Phase 3 SKYSCRAPER-06 Randomized Clinical Trial.** SKYSCRAPER-06 randomised 542 patients with untreated advanced nonsquamous NSCLC to tiragolumab (anti-TIGIT) plus atezolizumab and chemotherapy or placebo plus pembrolizumab and chemotherapy. The TIGIT arm was worse: median PFS 8.3 vs 9.9 months (HR 1.27) and OS 18.9 vs 23.1 months (HR 1.33), with grade 5 AEs 10.1% vs 5.9%; the trial was terminated. A clearly negative phase 3 for anti-TIGIT in first-line NSCLC. Limitations: short median follow-up (11.8 months), investigator-assessed PFS.  
  Socinski MA, Stahel R, Lee DH et al., JAMA Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42060297/

- **Deulorlatinib (TGRX-326) in ALK Gene Fusion Positive NSCLC After Failure of Second-Generation Inhibitors: A Single-Arm, Multicenter, Phase 2 Trial.** Pivotal single-arm phase 2 at 36 Chinese centres testing the third-generation ALK inhibitor deulorlatinib (60 mg daily) after failure of second-generation ALK TKIs (158 efficacy-evaluable patients). IRC-assessed ORR was 43.7% and median PFS 11.1 months; intracranial ORR 55.8% (n=43), and ORR 62.5% in 8 G1202R patients. Grade ≥3 treatment-related AEs occurred in 47.2%, with CNS AEs in 12.9%. Limitations: single-arm, China-only, very small G1202R subgroup.  
  Huang J, Chen G, Wang Z et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42031067/

- **JYP0322, a highly selective and brain-penetrant ROS1 inhibitor, overcomes ROS1G2032R resistance mutation in NSCLC: The first-in-human phase 1 trial.** First-in-human phase 1 trial of JYP0322, a brain-penetrant selective ROS1 inhibitor designed to overcome ROS1 G2032R, in 89 ROS1-fusion NSCLC patients (36% with brain metastases). Among 80 evaluable patients ORR was 95.7% in TKI-naive, 57.1% after ≥2 TKIs and 77.8% with G2032R; intracranial ORR 55.6%; TRK-related neurological AEs were low (dizziness 6.7%). Limitations: phase 1, small subgroups, early response data without mature PFS.  
  Ma Y, Xue J, Gao F et al., Cancer Cell, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42030931/

- **Blockade of Tumor-Intrinsic TGFβ Signaling Drives Hyperprogression in Small Cell Lung Cancer.** A clinical trial of the bifunctional PD-L1/TGFβ inhibitor bintrafusp alfa in 34 evaluable SCLC patients found 18% partial responses but 62% progression, with 38% of progressors meeting hyperprogression criteria; hyperprogression was also more frequent with bintrafusp alfa than PD-(L)1 blockade across 450 patients with other tumours. Functional work showed tumour-intrinsic TGFβ signalling restrains proliferation in a subset of SCLC, so its blockade can trigger hyperproliferation. Implies TGFβ-targeted immunotherapy needs biomarker selection. Limitations: small trial, mechanistic conclusions partly from models.  
  Schroeder BA, Mohindroo C, Meinhardt AL et al., Cancer Discov, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42018154/

- **Long-Term Efficacy and Safety of Taletrectinib in Patients With ROS1+ Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Results From the Phase II TRUST-I Study.** Long-term update of the phase 2 TRUST-I study of taletrectinib in Chinese patients with ROS1+ advanced NSCLC. In 103 TKI-naive patients (median follow-up 51 months) ORR was 90.3% with median DoR 49.7 and PFS 49.6 months and OS not reached; in 66 crizotinib-pretreated patients ORR was 51.5%, PFS 7.6 and OS 25.6 months. Supports durable efficacy of a next-generation ROS1 TKI. Limitations: single-arm, China-only.  
  Li W, Zhang Y, Fan H et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42013573/

- **First-in-Human, Phase I Study of Sigvotatug Vedotin, an Integrin Beta-6-Directed Antibody-Drug Conjugate: Results From Dose Expansion in Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.** Dose-expansion results of the first-in-human phase 1 SGNB6A-001 study of sigvotatug vedotin, an integrin beta-6-directed ADC, in 117 previously treated advanced NSCLC patients. ORR was 19% (median DoR 11.3 months) overall and 29% in nonsquamous taxane-naive disease; grade ≥3 TEAEs occurred in 48% (35% at the chosen 1.8 mg/kg AiBW q2w regimen). Establishes the dose for further trials of a new ADC target. Limitations: phase 1, no comparator, modest response rate.  
  Peters S, Piha-Paul SA, Sehgal K et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42008777/

- **First-Line Zongertinib in Advanced HER2-Mutant Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.** Cohort 2 of the phase 1a-1b Beamion LUNG-1 trial gave the HER2-selective, EGFR-sparing TKI zongertinib 120 mg to 74 untreated patients with HER2-mutant nonsquamous NSCLC. Confirmed ORR was 76%, median DoR 15.2 months and median PFS 14.4 months; in 30 patients with active brain metastases intracranial ORR was 47%; grade ≥3 treatment-related AEs 19%. Supports a first-line targeted option for HER2-mutant NSCLC. Limitations: single-arm, small cohorts, industry-funded.  
  Heymach JV, Yamamoto N, Girard N et al., N Engl J Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41985129/

- **SKYSCRAPER-02C: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study of Atezolizumab Plus Carboplatin and Etoposide With or Without Tiragolumab in Patients With Untreated Extensive-Stage SCLC in China.** SKYSCRAPER-02C is a double-blind phase 3 trial in China comparing tiragolumab plus atezolizumab and carboplatin-etoposide with placebo plus the same backbone in untreated extensive-stage SCLC (primary analysis set n=110, without brain metastases). Median PFS was 5.6 vs 5.4 months (HR 0.65, 95% CI 0.43-0.97) and median OS 18.7 vs 13.5 months (HR 0.89, 95% CI 0.56-1.40) after 26.7 months follow-up; inflamed molecular subtypes appeared to benefit. Adds to the largely negative anti-TIGIT data in lung cancer. Limitations: small regional trial, unstratified HRs, exploratory biomarker analysis in ~69 patients, and the global SKYSCRAPER-02 was negative.  
  Lu S, Fang J, Yu Y et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41950998/

- **Definitive Radiotherapy to the Primary Tumor in Stage IV NSCLC: A Consensus Statement From the International Association for the Study of Lung Cancer Advanced Radiation Technology Subcommittee.** IASLC Advanced Radiation Technology subcommittee consensus statement synthesising randomised studies (2010-2025) of radiotherapy to the primary tumour in stage IV NSCLC. It concludes that randomised phase III evidence supports early consolidative RT after induction systemic therapy in EGFR-mutant oligometastatic NSCLC, whereas evidence in patients without actionable alterations is insufficient for definitive recommendations; fractionation should be individualised. Relevant for local therapy in EGFR-mutant disease. Limitation: expert consensus on a limited randomised evidence base, with open questions on dose, timing and target volumes.  
  McMahon RA, Lee Min Chua K, Faivre-Finn C et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41934464/

- **Fulzerasib plus cetuximab in first-line KRASG12C-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer (KROCUS): a single-arm, multicentre, phase 1b/2 trial.** KROCUS is a single-arm phase 1b/2 trial at 30 European centres of first-line fulzerasib (KRAS G12C inhibitor) plus cetuximab in 47 treatment-naive KRAS G12C-mutated advanced NSCLC patients. Confirmed ORR was 69% (90% CI 56-80) at 12.8 months follow-up, with grade 3 TRAEs in 15% and no grade 4-5 events. Supports EGFR-antibody co-blockade with KRAS G12C inhibition; a phase 3 is planned. Limitations: small single-arm study, PFS/OS not reported in the abstract, sponsor-funded.  
  Gregorc V, Majem M, Lo Russo G et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41926959/

- **Durvalumab Consolidation in Limited-Stage SCLC: Outcomes by Prior Concurrent Chemoradiotherapy and Prophylactic Cranial Irradiation in the Phase 3 ADRIATIC Trial.** Exploratory prespecified subgroup analyses of the phase 3 ADRIATIC trial of consolidation durvalumab (n=264) vs placebo (n=266) after concurrent chemoradiotherapy in limited-stage SCLC. OS HRs favoured durvalumab across cisplatin- (0.82) and carboplatin-etoposide (0.56), once-daily (0.72) and twice-daily (0.68) RT, and with (0.75) or without (0.71) PCI, with no significant interactions. Supports durvalumab regardless of cCRT details. Limitation: exploratory subgroups with wide CIs, not powered individually.  
  Senan S, Cho BC, Laktionov KK et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41921748/
