- **Gotistobart or docetaxel in metastatic squamous non-small cell lung cancer: stage 1 of the randomized phase 3 PRESERVE-003 trial.** Stage 1 of the two-stage phase 3 PRESERVE-003 trial randomised 87 patients with metastatic squamous NSCLC progressing after PD-(L)1 inhibitor and platinum chemotherapy to gotistobart (pH-sensitive Treg-depleting anti-CTLA-4) or docetaxel. After 14.5 months median follow-up, median OS was not reached vs 10.0 months (HR 0.46, 95% CI 0.25-0.84, nominal P=0.01); grade ≥3 TRAEs 42% vs 49%. Promising in a population with poor options. Limitations: small dose-confirmation stage, nominal p-value, results preliminary pending stage 2.  
  Cho BC, Balaraman R, Chen HJ et al., Nat Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41896648/

- **Final Efficacy and Safety Data From the Phase I/II ARROW Study of Pralsetinib in Patients With Advanced RET Fusion-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.** Final analysis of the phase I/II ARROW study of pralsetinib 400 mg daily in 281 patients with RET fusion-positive NSCLC. ORR was 78% in treatment-naive and 63% in platinum-pretreated patients; median OS was 44.3 months overall (50.1 and 39.7 months respectively). Grade ≥3 TRAEs included anaemia 21% and hypertension 15%, with three treatment-related deaths. Confirms durable activity with long follow-up. Limitation: single-arm study without a comparator.  
  Besse B, Subbiah V, Curigliano G et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41886723/

- **Setidegrasib in Advanced Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer and Pancreatic Cancer.** Phase 1 dose-escalation/expansion study of setidegrasib (ASP3082), a first-in-class KRAS G12D-targeted protein degrader given weekly IV, in 203 patients with previously treated KRAS G12D tumours. Among 45 NSCLC patients at the 600 mg phase 2 dose, 36% had a partial response, median PFS was 8.3 months and 12-month OS 59%; grade ≥3 AEs occurred in 42% and infusion reactions in 80%. First clinical evidence for a degrader against this variant. Limitations: phase 1, small NSCLC cohort, no comparator.  
  Park W, Kasi A, Spira AI et al., N Engl J Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41879829/

- **AI-based chest X-ray prioritization in the lung cancer diagnostic pathway: the LungIMPACT randomized controlled trial.** LungIMPACT randomised (by day) AI prioritisation of 93,326 primary-care chest X-rays in the UK diagnostic pathway. Median time to CT was 53 days in both arms (ratio 0.97, P=0.31) and time to lung cancer diagnosis 44 vs 46 days (P=0.84), with no differences in stage, referral or treatment times; AI and radiology reports were discordant in 30.3%. A large negative RCT showing worklist prioritisation alone does not speed diagnosis. Limitations: day-level randomisation, UK primary-care setting, 558 cancers only.  
  Woznitza N, Smith L, Rawlinson J et al., Nat Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41876649/

- **Datopotamab Deruxtecan Plus Pembrolizumab With or Without Platinum-Based Chemotherapy for Advanced or Metastatic NSCLC: The Phase Ib TROPION-Lung02 Trial.** Phase Ib TROPION-Lung02 tested datopotamab deruxtecan plus pembrolizumab (doublet, n=70) or plus platinum chemotherapy (triplet, n=72) in advanced NSCLC without actionable alterations. In treatment-naive patients, confirmed ORR was 54.8% with doublet (median PFS 11.2 months, DoR 20.1 months) and 55.6% with triplet (PFS 6.8 months); grade ≥3 TRAEs 37.1% vs 59.7%. Responses occurred across PD-L1 levels and TROP2 NMR-positive patients trended better. Limitations: non-randomised phase Ib, small cohorts, exploratory biomarker.  
  Levy B, Paz-Ares L, Lin CC et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41871716/

- **Survival outcome of VATS compared with open lobectomy for lung cancer: an individual patient data meta-analysis of randomised trials.** Individual patient data meta-analysis of three randomised trials (n=1,185) comparing VATS with open lobectomy for early-stage NSCLC. OS favoured VATS (pooled HR 0.79, 95% CI 0.65-0.96), with similar DFS (HR 0.91) and no heterogeneity. Supports VATS as the preferred approach when feasible. Limitations: only three trials, none individually powered for survival.  
  Harris RA, Law JJ, Hao L et al., Lancet, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41864749/

- **Benmelstobart plus anlotinib versus pembrolizumab as first-line treatment for PD-L1-positive, advanced non-small-cell lung cancer (CAMPASS): a blinded, randomised, controlled, phase 3 trial.** CAMPASS is a blinded phase 3 trial at 79 Chinese centres randomising 531 patients (2:1) with untreated PD-L1 ≥1% driver-negative advanced NSCLC to benmelstobart (anti-PD-L1) plus anlotinib or pembrolizumab plus placebo. Median PFS was 11.0 vs 7.1 months (HR 0.70, 95% CI 0.54-0.90), but grade ≥3 TRAEs were 59% vs 29% (hypertension 26%), with fatal haemoptysis in two patients. Adds phase 3 evidence for PD-(L)1 plus antiangiogenic combinations. Limitations: China-only, 85% male, short follow-up (~11 months), OS immature, sponsor-funded.  
  Zhong H, Wang J, Yang R et al., Lancet Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41825453/

- **First-Line Tislelizumab Plus Chemotherapy Versus Placebo Plus Chemotherapy in Extensive-Stage SCLC: A Long-Term Survival and Programmed Death-Ligand 1 Subgroup Analysis From the Randomized, Phase 3 RATIONALE-312 Trial.** Long-term follow-up of the phase 3 RATIONALE-312 trial of first-line tislelizumab plus platinum-etoposide (n=227) vs placebo plus chemotherapy (n=230) in extensive-stage SCLC. With ~37-40 months median follow-up, median OS was 15.5 vs 13.5 months (HR 0.78, 95% CI 0.63-0.95), consistent across PD-L1 subgroups, with no new safety signals. Confirms durable benefit of adding a PD-1 inhibitor. Limitations: modest absolute OS gain, PD-L1 analyses exploratory.  
  Fan Y, Zhao Y, Huang D et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41796863/

- **Combination of Lurbinectedin Plus Irinotecan: Preclinical and Early Clinical Results in Patients With Relapsed SCLC.** Combined preclinical work (cell lines, organoids, xenografts) and a phase I-II trial of lurbinectedin plus irinotecan; the recommended dose was lurbinectedin 2.0 mg/m2 D1 plus irinotecan 75 mg/m2 D1/D8 q3w with growth-factor support. Among 21 relapsed SCLC patients with measurable disease at that dose, ORR was 61.9%, median PFS 7.2 months and OS 12.3 months; myelosuppression was the main toxicity. Promising for second-line SCLC. Limitations: very small, non-randomised cohort.  
  Ponce S, Ruiz-Torres M, Falcón A et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41791703/

- **HS-20093, a B7-H3-targeted antibody-drug conjugate in lung cancer: Results from the ARTEMIS-001 phase 1a/b trial.** Phase 1a/b ARTEMIS-001 of the B7-H3-directed ADC HS-20093 in 306 pretreated solid-tumour patients, including 236 with lung cancer at 8 or 10 mg/kg. Confirmed ORR was 52.3% in ES-SCLC (n=65) and 22.4% in NSCLC (n=152); grade ≥3 neutropenia was 25.5% vs 50.5% by dose, treatment-related ILD 3.4% and fatal AEs 3.8%. 8 mg/kg was chosen for phase 3. Limitations: phase 1, single-arm, notable haematological toxicity.  
  Duan J, Sun Y, Wang Q et al., Cancer Cell, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41791381/

- **ELIOS: A Multicenter, Molecular Profiling Study of Patients with EGFR-Mutant Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Treated with First-Line Osimertinib.** ELIOS prospectively obtained paired pre-treatment and post-progression biopsies from patients with EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC on first-line osimertinib (154 enrolled; 52 with paired tissue NGS). The most common acquired alterations were MET amplification (17%), CDKN2A/B deletion (15%), MTAP loss (13%) and EGFR C797S (13%); TROP2 stayed highly expressed regardless of genotype, and 82% had potential resistance alterations when tissue and plasma were combined. Informs post-osimertinib strategies (MET-directed, TROP2 ADCs). Limitations: only a third of patients had paired tissue, small proteogenomic subsets.  
  Piotrowska Z, Ahn MJ, Voon PJ et al., Cancer Discov, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41790042/

- **Osimertinib plus datopotamab deruxtecan in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC after progression on first-line osimertinib: ORCHARD.** Final data from the osimertinib plus datopotamab deruxtecan module of the phase II ORCHARD platform in EGFR-mutated NSCLC progressing on first-line osimertinib (n=69). With Dato-DXd 4 mg/kg (n=35) and 6 mg/kg (n=34), ORR was 43% and 36%, median PFS 9.5 and 11.7 months, and median OS 19.8 and 26.2 months; grade ≥3 AEs were 49% and 76% and adjudicated ILD 3% and 15%. Supports 6 mg/kg as the combination dose in post-osimertinib disease. Limitations: small non-randomised cohorts with 80% CIs, notable toxicity at the higher dose.  
  Riess JW, Yu HA, Le X et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41780641/

- **Izalontamab Brengitecan in Locally Advanced or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Actionable Genomic Alterations Outside of Classical EGFR Mutations: A Phase Ib Study.** Phase Ib study of izalontamab brengitecan (an EGFRxHER3 bispecific ADC) 2.5 mg/kg D1/D8 q3w in 83 pretreated NSCLC patients with actionable alterations other than classical EGFR mutations. Confirmed ORR was 39.7% and median PFS 7.0 months; ORR was 69.2% (PFS 10.5 months) in EGFR exon20ins/nonclassical mutations (n=14) and 52.9% in HER2-mutant disease. Grade ≥3 thrombocytopenia occurred in 51.8%. A new bispecific ADC with activity in rare EGFR subtypes. Limitations: phase Ib, small cohorts, OS immature, high haematological toxicity.  
  Zhou H, Zhao H, Hou X et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41779981/

- **Four-Year Outcomes of First-Line Nivolumab Plus Ipilimumab for 6 Months Versus Continuation in Patients With Advanced NSCLC: Results of the Randomized IFCT-1701 "DICIPLE" Phase III Trial.** IFCT-1701 DICIPLE was a randomised noninferiority phase III trial in which patients with metastatic NSCLC and disease control after 6 months of nivolumab-ipilimumab were randomised to continue or stop (resuming at progression). Only 71 of 265 enrolled patients were randomised before premature interruption; at 4 years, median PFS was 18.7 months (continuation) vs not reached (stop), 18-month OS 80.6% vs 93.8%, grade 3-5 TRAEs 54.3% vs 23.5%, and QoL deterioration was delayed (HR 0.36). Supports stopping dual ICI at 6 months in responders. Limitations: very small randomised population, early termination, per-protocol analysis.  
  Zalcman G, Madroszyk A, Guenzi E et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41690366/

- **Patient-Reported Outcomes With Consolidation Durvalumab Versus Placebo After Concurrent Chemoradiotherapy in Limited-Stage SCLC: Results From the Phase 3 ADRIATIC Trial.** Secondary patient-reported outcome analysis of the phase 3 ADRIATIC trial comparing consolidation durvalumab (n=264) with placebo (n=266) after concurrent chemoradiotherapy in limited-stage SCLC, using EORTC QLQ-C30/LC13. Mean changes in key scales over 24 months were small and not clinically meaningful, with no between-arm differences in time to deterioration except arm/shoulder pain (HR 0.70 favouring durvalumab). Shows the survival benefit does not come at a QoL cost. Limitations: analyses not alpha-controlled, open to missing-data bias.  
  Novello S, Spigel DR, Fang J et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41621752/

- **Izalontamab brengitecan (Iza-bren; BL-B01D1), a first-in-class EGFR-HER3 bispecific antibody-drug conjugate, for patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC: pooled analysis of phase I and phase II trials.** Exploratory post hoc pooled analysis of a phase Ia/Ib and a phase II trial of izalontamab brengitecan (EGFR-HER3 bispecific ADC) in 171 patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC progressing on EGFR TKIs (median follow-up 20.5 months). cORR was 47.4%, median PFS 6.9 months and median OS 24.8 months; in 50 chemotherapy-naive post-TKI patients at the RP2D, cORR was 56.0% and PFS 12.5 months. Grade ≥3 TRAEs occurred in 70.2% (mostly haematological), ILD 0.6%. A new post-osimertinib option under randomised testing. Limitations: post hoc pooling of non-randomised trials, high haematological toxicity, China-based.  
  Hong SD, Wang YS, Zhao HY et al., Ann Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41611210/

- **Fecal microbiota transplantation plus immunotherapy in non-small cell lung cancer and melanoma: the phase 2 FMT-LUMINate trial.** FMT-LUMINate is an open-label phase 2 trial of a single oral-capsule healthy-donor FMT before first-line anti-PD-1 in NSCLC (n=20) or dual ICI in melanoma (n=20). ORR in NSCLC was 80% (16/20), meeting the primary endpoint, with no grade ≥3 AEs; responders showed loss of specific baseline taxa (e.g. Enterocloster species), and reintroducing them abolished ICI benefit in mice. Supports microbiome modulation as an ICI adjunct. Limitations: very small single-arm cohort without control, PD-L1 selection not described in the abstract.  
  Duttagupta S, Messaoudene M, Hunter S et al., Nat Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41606121/

- **Osimertinib and Stereotactic Radiosurgery for Brain Metastases in EGFR-Mutated Lung Cancer: The STARLET Joint Analysis of OUTRUN and LUOSICNS Randomized Trials.** STARLET prospectively pooled two randomised phase II trials (OUTRUN, LUOSICNS) of upfront stereotactic radiosurgery plus osimertinib vs osimertinib alone in 79 patients with EGFR-mutant NSCLC and up to ten brain metastases. After 39 months median follow-up, 12-month intracranial PFS was not significantly different (difference 11%, p=0.31; median 21.9 vs 17.2 months); median OS was 46.1 vs 29.1 months and grade 3/4 radionecrosis 5% with SRS. First randomised data supporting osimertinib alone upfront in minimally symptomatic low-burden brain metastases (CNS efficacy). Limitations: small sample, underpowered for OS, phase II.  
  Lee CK, Lefresne S, Soon YY et al., J Thorac Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41534788/

- **Adjuvant Durvalumab in Completely Resected Early-Stage Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.** The CCTG BR.31 phase 3 trial randomised 1,415 patients (2:1) with completely resected stage IB (≥4 cm)-IIIA NSCLC to adjuvant durvalumab or placebo for 12 cycles; 1,219 had EGFR-/ALK- tumours. After 60 months median follow-up, DFS was not improved in the primary PD-L1 ≥25% population (HR 0.93, P=.64) or in secondary populations, while grade 3-4 AEs were higher (26% vs 20%). A negative trial adding to conflicting adjuvant immunotherapy evidence. Limitation: AJCC 7th staging and optional adjuvant chemotherapy; OS results not detailed in the abstract.  
  Goss GD, Darling GE, Westeel V et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41529222/

- **Chemoradiation ± Atezolizumab in Limited-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: Results of NRG Oncology/Alliance LU005.** NRG Oncology/Alliance LU005 is an open-label international phase III trial in limited-stage SCLC randomising patients after one chemotherapy cycle to chemoradiation alone or with concurrent and adjuvant atezolizumab. Median OS was 36.1 vs 31.1 months (HR 1.03) and PFS 11.4 vs 12.1 months (HR 0.98); no unexpected safety signals. A negative result for concurrent PD-L1 blockade, contrasting with consolidation durvalumab in ADRIATIC. Limitation: number randomised not given in the abstract; open-label.  
  Higgins KA, Hu C, Ross HJ et al., J Clin Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41529214/

### lung-nonpharm

- **A 12-Week Structured Antioxidant-Focused Dietary Intervention Improves Cognitive Function and Oxidative Stress Biomarkers in Lung Cancer Patients with Cancer-Related Cognitive Impairment: A Randomized Controlled Trial.** Assessor-blinded randomised controlled trial in 100 lung cancer patients with cancer-related cognitive impairment comparing a 12-week antioxidant-focused dietary intervention with standard dietary counselling. The intervention improved MoCA scores (beta 2.63, 95% CI 0.83-4.43), dietary oxidative balance and physical/mental QoL, raised glutathione and SOD and lowered malondialdehyde, and fewer participants still met CRCI criteria at 12 weeks (60.0% vs 93.5%, complete-case n=91). It is a randomised non-drug intervention with patient-relevant cognitive and QoL outcomes. Limitations: single trial of modest size, short 12-week follow-up, mostly male participants, and dietary interventions cannot be blinded to participants.  
  Huang Z, Cheng X, He J et al., Antioxidants (Basel), 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42650196/

- **Behavioral speech therapy intervention can improve the postoperative pain and performance status of lung cancer patients undergoing thoracoscopic surgery.** Secondary analysis of a registered trial (ChiCTR2400084313, originally on postoperative cough) in 112 patients undergoing thoracoscopic lung cancer surgery, comparing routine care with routine care plus behavioural speech therapy, with blinded outcome assessment. Pain (VAS) was lower with BST at 1 month (MD -0.63) and 3 months (MD -0.88), and ECOG performance status was better at 1 month (MD -0.27) but not significantly at 3 months. It offers randomised evidence for a low-risk non-drug adjunct to postoperative recovery. Limitations: secondary analysis of outcomes the trial was not designed for, single trial of modest size, small absolute differences and short follow-up.  
  Zhang H, Jin Y, Song X et al., Support Care Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42611082/

- **Effectiveness of an Interdisciplinary Integrated Care Model on Perioperative Outcomes in Patients Undergoing Lung Cancer Surgery: A Randomized Controlled Trial.** This single-centre randomised trial assigned 240 patients having lung cancer surgery to standard care or an interdisciplinary model (120 per arm) combining preoperative pulmonary rehabilitation, psychological support, nutritional optimisation and enhanced-recovery protocols. In the intention-to-treat analysis the intervention shortened hospital stay (7.43 vs 9.02 days; mean difference 1.59 days, 95% CI 1.07-2.12) and cut postoperative pulmonary complications (12.0% vs 26.1%; RR 0.46). It also gave clinically meaningful 6-minute-walk gains at 1 and 3 months (about 58-62 m) and lower HADS anxiety and depression scores. This is randomised evidence for a multicomponent prehabilitation package, but the trial was single-centre, the intervention could not be blinded, there was a baseline FEV1 imbalance, and the multicomponent design cannot show which element drove the benefit; survival was not reported.  
  Chen X, Zhang N, Zhang X et al., ANZ J Surg, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42503044/

- **Holistic oncologic management improves quality of life in driver gene negative advanced NSCLC a latent growth curve modeling study.** This single-centre randomised controlled trial in Chongqing enrolled patients with driver-gene-negative advanced NSCLC (81 vs 77). It compared 24 weeks of holistic integrative oncologic management, targeting quality of life, depression, pain and nutrition, with control care. Latent growth curve modelling of FACT-L showed significantly better quality-of-life trajectories, less cancer-related pain, and improved nutritional status and mood with the intervention, with larger gains in younger patients. It is randomised evidence for a multicomponent supportive-care model, but the abstract gives no effect sizes, the trial was single-centre and unblinded, it was registered retrospectively, and survival was not reported.  
  Ma LL, Zhang JH, Lei L et al., Sci Rep, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42437787/

- **Effects of Telerehabilitation Based on Motion Recognition Technology on Exercise Endurance of Patients With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer After Surgery: Single-Center, Prospective, Open-Label, Randomized Controlled Trial.** This single-centre, open-label randomised trial (JMIR mHealth uHealth) compared 4 weeks of telerehabilitation with live motion-capture feedback against WeChat video-guided training without feedback in patients discharged after curative NSCLC resection. The motion-recognition arm gained more on 6-minute walk distance (mean difference 32 m, 95% CI 5.4-58.6), FVC (+476 mL), FEV1 (+346 mL), Timed Up and Go (-1.6 s) and total FACT-L (+13 points). Adherence was higher (72% vs 40%) and there were no exercise-related adverse events. It is randomised evidence for supervised remote exercise after lung surgery, but the abstract does not give the sample size, the trial was single-centre and unblinded, the comparator was itself an exercise programme, follow-up ended 4 weeks after the intervention, and the spirometry differences are unusually large.  
  Chen L, Xu X, Yang X et al., JMIR Mhealth Uhealth, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42373087/

- **Effects of CALM-based psycho-oncological counseling on the emotional well-being of relatives of people living with lung cancer: Results of a randomized controlled trial.** Multicentre RCT of CALM-based psycho-oncological counselling (3-6 sessions) versus usual care for relatives of people with lung cancer, with relatives' anxiety at 6 weeks as the primary outcome. Among 86 relatives (40 intervention, 46 control) there were no between-group effects on anxiety, depression, distress or QoL; only within-group decreases in depression and distress were seen in the intervention arm, and patients whose relatives attended at least one session had reduced distress. It is one of few randomised psychosocial trials targeting lung cancer caregivers, but it is small, the primary endpoint was negative, and follow-up was only 6 weeks.  
  Dusel J, Meng K, Faßler P et al., J Psychosoc Oncol, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42186802/

- **Perioperative Normobaric Hyperoxia Combined With Pulmonary Rehabilitation Intervention for Enhanced Short-Term Recovery in Lung Cancer Surgery.** Single-centre RCT of perioperative normobaric hyperoxia plus pulmonary rehabilitation versus control in 100 patients scheduled for VATS for pulmonary nodules/lung cancer (84 completed), with postoperative length of stay and hospital costs as primary outcomes. The intervention group had a shorter postoperative stay (median 2 days) and lower total costs, and better activities-of-daily-living scores after surgery and at discharge, but no difference in pulmonary complications, inflammatory markers, or 30-day/6-month readmissions. It is a randomised test of a perioperative rehabilitation strategy, but it is modest in size with 16% dropout, registered in 2018, combines two interventions so their effects cannot be separated, and its benefits are limited to short-term process outcomes.  
  Zhou M, Liu J, Li D et al., Thorac Cancer, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42120327/

- **Impact of the Oncologist's Recommendation on Exercise Levels and Quality of Life in Patients With Lung Cancer: The ORE Randomized Controlled Trial.** Single-blind, three-arm RCT (ORE, NCT05497544) in 91 patients with stage I-IV lung cancer comparing usual care, a verbal and written exercise recommendation from the oncologist, or the recommendation plus a printed exercise guidebook. At 8 weeks the recommendation-only arm increased total physical activity by about 270 min/week versus controls (p=0.03), the guidebook arm showed a borderline increase (+251 min/week, p=0.05), sedentary behaviour did not change, and QoL effects were limited to isolated subscales. It is randomised evidence that a cheap oncologist-delivered prompt can raise activity in lung cancer, but the trial is small, activity was likely self-reported, and 8-week follow-up cannot show effects on survival or treatment tolerance.  
  Avancini A, Belluomini L, Giannarelli D et al., Cancer Med, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42036773/

- **Pre-diagnosis recreational physical activity and lung cancer survival within the California teachers study.** Prospective cohort analysis of 1,768 women diagnosed with lung cancer in the California Teachers Study (1995-2019), relating pre-diagnosis lifetime and recent moderate/strenuous recreational physical activity to lung cancer-specific mortality. Overall, most activity measures were not associated with mortality, but associations varied by smoking: more lifetime moderate activity was linked to lower mortality among ever/former smokers (HR 0.77/0.63), and lifetime strenuous activity to lower mortality in never smokers (HR 0.69) but higher mortality in ever/former smokers (HR 1.24/1.39). It is a large prospective cohort on activity and lung cancer survival, but activity was self-reported at enrolment, possibly years before diagnosis, only women were included, the subgroup findings are inconsistent, and post-diagnosis activity and treatment were not captured.  
  Cauble EL, Blanchard M, Reynolds P et al., Cancer Causes Control, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42018036/

- **Effect of preoperative respiratory prehabilitation on cardiopulmonary function in patients undergoing lung resection for non-small cell lung cancer: a randomized controlled trial.** Single-centre exploratory RCT in 120 patients scheduled for curative-intent lobectomy or wedge resection for suspected NSCLC, comparing routine care with routine care plus a 7-day preoperative respiratory prehabilitation programme. On postoperative day 7 the prehabilitation group had higher FEV1 (1.86 vs 1.55 L), FVC (2.30 vs 1.78 L), PEF and cardiopulmonary exercise measures (all p<=0.003), and fewer complications (6.7% vs 18.3%), although that difference was not significant (p=0.053). It is randomised evidence that even a one-week prehabilitation programme improves early postoperative function, but it is a single-centre trial whose primary endpoint was an early surrogate (day-7 spirometry), it was not powered for complications, and it had no longer-term follow-up.  
  Huang F, Kong P, Liu J et al., J Cardiothorac Surg, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42010447/

- **Effectiveness of mindfulness-based stress reduction in managing fatigue, anxiety, and depression among non-small cell lung cancer patients receiving surgery and adjuvant chemotherapy: A randomized controlled trial.** RCT in 80 NSCLC patients undergoing surgery and adjuvant chemotherapy, comparing an 8-week mindfulness-based stress reduction (MBSR) programme with standard care, assessing fatigue, anxiety, depression, self-efficacy and mindfulness from before surgery to 3 months post-intervention. The MBSR group reported significantly lower anxiety and depression from week 4 onward (p<0.01), and fatigue returned near baseline by 3 months while staying elevated in controls (p<0.05), together with better self-efficacy. It adds randomised evidence for a psychosocial intervention during curative-intent treatment, but it is small and unblinded, all outcomes were self-reported, effect sizes are not given in the abstract, and follow-up ends at 3 months.  
  Liu X, Chen H, Cheng Y et al., Medicine (Baltimore), 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41931358/

## Medium

- EGFR-Degrading PROTACs and Calcium Peroxide Co-loaded Liposomes: Enabling EGFR Degradation and Hypoxia Alleviation as Dual Safeguards for NSCLC Therapy. (ACS Appl Mater Interfaces). Preclinical liposomal co-delivery of an EGFR-degrading PROTAC with an oxygen supplier improved EGFR degradation and tumour inhibition in NSCLC animal models, but has no clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42679142/
- DNA and RNA EGFR Variant Allele Fractions and Clinical Outcomes in Lung Adenocarcinoma. (J Thorac Oncol). JTO multi-omics study linking DNA/RNA EGFR variant allele fractions to relapse and TKI PFS in two modest cohorts (n=133 and 54), hypothesis-generating for TKI timing. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42674257/
- Delayed Occurrence of Lung Cancer Leptomeningeal Metastasis and Era-Associated Post-LM Survival: A 14-Year Single-Center Cohort Study. (Thorac Cancer). 14-year single-centre cohort of 201 patients with leptomeningeal metastasis finding post-LM third-generation EGFR-TKI exposure associated with lower mortality (aHR 0.385), with acknowledged confounding. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42670654/
- Risk-adapted first-line therapy selection in advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC: a practical clinical algorithm integrating tumor biology and patient factors. (Med Oncol). Review proposing a risk-adapted first-line algorithm for choosing between osimertinib monotherapy and FLAURA2/MARIPOSA combinations, synthesising existing evidence without new data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42667515/
- Adding immunotherapy to chemotherapy may improve survival after SCLC transformation in EGFR-mutant NSCLC: a multicenter real-world study. (Lung Cancer). 26-centre retrospective cohort of 59 patients with SCLC transformation after EGFR-TKI, where adding immunotherapy to carboplatin-etoposide was associated with longer post-transformation OS (17 vs 9 months). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42664544/
- Do Generic EGFR-TKIs Perform as Well as Innovators? Real-World Evidence from Gefitinib-Treated EGFR-Mutated NSCLC in Indonesia. (Asian Pac J Cancer Prev). Indonesian two-centre retrospective cohort (n=192) comparing generic vs innovator gefitinib, relevant to access/outcome differences between countries. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42663218/
- The impact of EGFR subtype combined with TP53 co-mutation status on survival outcomes with front-line osimertinib in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). (Lung Cancer). Large US clinical-genomic real-world cohort (n=606) showing L858R plus TP53 co-mutation predicts much shorter rwPFS and OS on front-line osimertinib. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42660014/
- Global pharmacovigilance analysis of dermatologic toxicity in EGFR-TKI-treated NSCLC: a cross-database real-world study. (Front Pharmacol). Large pharmacovigilance analysis (96,386 reports, VigiAccess and EudraVigilance) comparing dermatologic toxicity profiles across five EGFR-TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42656595/
- Targeting EGFR in lung cancer: lessons in signal transduction and treatment-induced mutagenesis. (Philos Trans R Soc Lond B Biol Sci). Review on drug-tolerant persisters and treatment-induced mutagenesis as drivers of EGFR-TKI resistance, from the 'EGFR after 40 years' issue, without new data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42656150/
- Towards better outcomes for epidermal growth factor receptor L858R+ lung cancer patients: mutant-selective allosteric inhibitors and the potential for double-drugging. (Philos Trans R Soc Lond B Biol Sci). Review of mutant-selective allosteric EGFR inhibitors (e.g. EAI-432) for L858R NSCLC and co-dosing with osimertinib, a next-generation strategy still preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42656147/
- Anti-TROP2 Antibody Drug Conjugates in EGFR-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Biological Rationale and Clinical Challenges. (Pharmaceutics). Review critically appraising clinical data for three TROP2-directed ADCs in EGFR-mutant NSCLC after TKI progression. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42654023/
- LINC00511 drives osimertinib resistance in LUAD via PERK kinase: A clinical biomarker with enzymatic therapeutic potential. (Cell Signal). Cell-line and xenograft study implicating LINC00511/PERK-Nrf2 in osimertinib resistance, with only limited patient blood validation. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42648586/
- Targeting EP2 overcomes osimertinib-tolerant persisters through metabolic and inflammatory reprogramming in non-small cell lung cancer. (Int Immunopharmacol). Preclinical study showing EP2 drives osimertinib-tolerant persister cells via GYS1/succinate/HIF-1α and that EP2 inhibition reverses this in xenografts. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42647987/
- Pragmatic Management of EGFR-Mutant NSCLC After Progression on Osimertinib: Canadian Expert Perspectives. (Curr Oncol). Canadian expert perspective on post-osimertinib management highlighting uneven provincial access to NGS and newer therapies, relevant to access differences. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42645370/
- Therapeutic evolution: Adoption of newly approved systemic therapies in resected stage IB-IIIA NSCLC in the United States. (Lung Cancer). Large US real-world cohort (n=14,638) describing uptake of adjuvant immunotherapy and osimertinib in resected stage IB-IIIA NSCLC from 2019 to 2023. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636546/
- Osimertinib-Associated Cardiotoxicity in EGFR-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Mechanisms, Phenotypes, and Surveillance. (Cardiol Rev). Mechanistic review of osimertinib-associated cardiotoxicity proposing surveillance, relevant as osimertinib exposure lengthens in adjuvant use. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42636333/
- PARP inhibition enhances the antitumor activity of HER3-DXd in non-small cell lung cancer. (Cell Rep Med). Preclinical Cell Reports Medicine study showing PARP inhibitors synergise with HER3-DXd in EGFR- and KRAS-mutant NSCLC models in vivo, a credible combination strategy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42628527/
- Genomic Profiling, Risk Stratification, and Post-Transformation Treatment Outcomes in Patients with Transformed Small-Cell Lung Cancer: A Multicenter Analysis. (Lung Cancer). Five-centre retrospective cohort of 163 transformed-SCLC patients with paired genomics and comparison of post-transformation regimens (EGFR-TKI plus chemotherapy gave longest PFS). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42628300/
- PIK3CA in the transformation of EGFR‑mutant lung adenocarcinoma to small cell lung cancer following acquired targeted therapy resistance. (Chin Med J (Engl)). Report on PIK3CA in EGFR-mutant adenocarcinoma transformation to SCLC after TKI resistance; no abstract to judge data size or design. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42624799/
- A novel peptide encoded by circTLL1 drives osimertinib resistance in lung cancer by modulating the NT5C2/Ras/PI3K axis. (Cell Signal). Preclinical study identifying a circTLL1-encoded peptide driving osimertinib resistance via NT5C2/Ras/PI3K in cell lines and xenografts. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42612716/
- Distinct spatial immune microenvironment features of different EGFR mutation subtypes in early-stage lung adenocarcinoma. (Lung Cancer). Spatial immune profiling of 144 early-stage EGFR-mutant LUADs showing L858R and rare variants are more T-cell infiltrated than 19del, hypothesis-generating for perioperative therapy choice. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42612510/
- Polycomb Scaffolding Subunit EED Supports the SCLC Neuroendocrine Identity and EGFR-Mutant LUAD to SCLC Transformation. (J Clin Invest). JCI GEMM study showing PRC2 scaffold EED loss prevents SCLC histology emerging after EGFR withdrawal, a mechanistic insight into transformation without clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42611544/
- Squamous transformation of EGFR-mutant lung adenocarcinoma after EGFR-TKI therapy: clonal continuity, tissue-based diagnosis, and therapeutic evidence gaps. (Front Oncol). Focused review of squamous transformation after EGFR-TKI, separating direct clinical evidence from translational data and identifying treatment evidence gaps. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42609500/
- CTPS1 promotes lung adenocarcinoma progression as a targetable vulnerability. (Br J Cancer). Preclinical study showing CTPS1 inhibitor STP938 enhanced osimertinib efficacy in EGFR-mutant xenografts. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42608454/
- Phase II study of olaparib plus durvalumab in EGFR-mutant NSCLC transformed to small cell lung cancer. (Oncologist). Single-arm phase II of olaparib plus durvalumab in SCLC-transformed EGFR-mutant NSCLC was stopped early for slow accrual after 4 patients, with ORR 0% and median PFS 1.75 months. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42605066/
- Recent advances in synthetic strategies of fourth-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors under clinical trials for the treatment of resistant non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). (Bioorg Chem). Review of synthetic routes and pharmacology of clinical-stage fourth-generation EGFR-TKIs targeting C797S (BLU-945, BDTX-1535, etc.), chemistry-focused. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42603542/
- Treatment prioritization of chemotherapy-anchored regimens after EGFR-TKI failure in EGFR-mutant NSCLC: a systematic review, pairwise meta-analysis and Bayesian network meta-analysis with probabilistic multi-criteria decision analysis. (Front Immunol). Systematic review and Bayesian network meta-analysis of RCTs comparing chemotherapy-anchored regimens after EGFR-TKI failure, relevant to post-osimertinib sequencing. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42602055/
- Disparities in Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Mutation Testing for Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Insights From the National Cancer Database. (J Surg Res). NCDB analysis of 2021-2022 NSCLC cases quantifying disparities in EGFR mutation testing after adjuvant osimertinib approval. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42600435/
- Lung-molGPA may stratify the prognostic impact of TP53 co-mutation in EGFR-mutant lung adenocarcinoma with brain metastases: a multi-center retrospective analysis. (Ther Adv Med Oncol). Multicentre retrospective cohort (n=218) of EGFR-mutant LUAD with brain metastases showing Lung-molGPA stratifies the prognostic impact of TP53 co-mutation on third-generation TKI outcomes. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42597873/
- Population Pharmacokinetics and Exposure-Response Analyses to Support Cofetuzumab Pelidotin (ABBV-647) Dose Optimization in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Patients. (Clin Pharmacol Ther). Population PK and exposure-response analysis supporting dose optimisation of the PTK7 ADC cofetuzumab pelidotin in mostly EGFR wild-type NSCLC. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42596608/
- Mitochondrial Morphology Dynamics Remodel Metabolism and Affect TKI Sensitivity in EGFR-Mutated Lung Cancer. (Cancer Sci). Mouse-model study showing early mitochondrial fission and OXPHOS remodelling after TKI, with fission inhibition sensitising EGFR-mutant cells to TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42592894/
- Clinical Efficacy and Safety of Icotinib as Adjuvant Therapy for Resected Stage IA Lung Adenocarcinoma: A Real-world Study. (Clin Lung Cancer). Propensity-matched real-world study (89 pairs) suggesting lower relapse with adjuvant icotinib in resected stage IA adenocarcinoma, a setting outside current indications. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42586029/
- Postoperative recurrence defines a 3-year high-risk window in patients with EGFR-mutant lung adenocarcinoma: a multicenter cohort study. (J Thorac Dis). Four-centre Japanese cohort of 787 resected patients showing EGFR-mutant recurrence concentrates in a 3-year high-risk window, relevant to interpreting adjuvant TKI duration. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42583347/
- Early EGFR Inhibition Triggers Actionable Tumor and Microenvironmental Remodeling in Lung Cancer. (Cancer Res Commun). Window study of 13 patients given 4 weeks of neoadjuvant gefitinib with multiregion sequencing, showing rapid tumour and microenvironment remodelling without T790M emergence. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42579877/
- Nivolumab plus ipilimumab-based treatment in Japanese patients with metastatic non-small cell lung cancer and tumor PD-L1 < 1%: a pooled analysis. (Int J Clin Oncol). Pooled Japanese PD-L1 <1% subgroup (n=75) of CheckMate 227/9LA showing longer OS with nivolumab-ipilimumab (HR 0.46), a small post hoc subgroup excluding EGFR/ALK. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42579028/
- Cost-effectiveness analysis of sacituzumab tirumotecan vs. chemotherapy for patients with EGFR-TKI-resistant, EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer in China. (Front Public Health). Chinese cost-effectiveness model finding sac-TMT after EGFR-TKI failure not cost-effective (ICER $33,515/QALY) unless the price falls about 15%, relevant to access. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42577174/
- Mycoplasma infection drives EGFR-TKI resistance through convergent transcriptomic reprogramming in non-small cell lung cancer. (iScience). Preclinical study showing mycoplasma infection reduces osimertinib sensitivity via sustained ERK/AKT/NF-κB, partly reversed by antibiotics, with supporting survival associations. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42572587/
- Comparative Treatment Outcomes Across Actionable Genomic Alterations in NSCLC: A Large Multicenter Real-World Study. (JTO Clin Res Rep). Multicentre Japanese real-world cohort (n=810) comparing targeted-therapy outcomes across EGFR, fusion-driven and other actionable alterations. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42569082/
- De-escalation of Adjuvant EGFR-TKI in Stage IB EGFR-Mutated Non-small-cell Lung Cancer: A Real-World Study. (Ann Surg Oncol). Propensity-matched real-world study of 340 resected stage IB EGFR-mutant patients in which adjuvant TKI improved 5-year DFS (82.8% vs 76.1%) but not OS. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42563120/
- A pathway-dependency framework for acquired resistance to targeted therapy in EGFR-mutant, KRAS G12C-mutant, and BRAF V600E-mutant NSCLC. (Pharmacol Res). Conceptual review proposing a MAPK/PI3K pathway-dependency framework for post-progression decisions in EGFR-, KRAS G12C- and BRAF-mutant NSCLC. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42562082/
- Incidence of Radiation Pneumonitis Following EGFR-TKI Combined With Thoracic Radiotherapy in Unresectable Stage III or IV Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis. (Clin Lung Cancer). Meta-analysis of 20 studies (n=693) estimating a 62% pooled incidence of radiation pneumonitis (5.3% grade 3-5) with EGFR-TKI plus thoracic radiotherapy, from mostly small studies. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42561633/
- Novel 2-amino-4-(1H-imidazol)pyridine derivatives as potent brain-penetrant EGFR mutant inhibitors: Design, synthesis, biological evaluation, and molecular modeling study. (Bioorg Chem). Medicinal-chemistry study of a brain-penetrant compound (8m) active against EGFR L858R/T790M/C797S with xenograft and intracranial model efficacy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42561559/
- Real-world analysis of perioperative strategies for driver gene-positive resectable non-small cell lung cancer. (Ther Adv Med Oncol). Two-centre retrospective cohort (n=290) comparing neoadjuvant targeted therapy, chemoimmunotherapy, chemotherapy and upfront surgery in driver-positive resectable NSCLC. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42558961/
- Development of a structurally distinct TopBP1 inhibitor that enhances PARP blockade and reverses osimertinib resistance. (Sci Adv). Science Advances study of a new TopBP1 inhibitor (CS18) that overcame osimertinib resistance in refractory NSCLC in vivo, still preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42555732/
- Aumolertinib Versus Osimertinib as First-Line Treatment for EGFR-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Retrospective Real-World Multicenter Study. (Int J Cancer). Four-centre retrospective head-to-head (n=109) suggesting longer PFS with first-line aumolertinib than osimertinib (34.4 vs 26.9 months), limited by small size and non-randomised design. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42552814/
- CCL20 Released by Drug-Tolerant Persisters Impairs Immunotherapy in EGFR-Mutant Lung Adenocarcinoma. (MedComm (2020)). Humanised-mouse study (n=6/group) showing drug-tolerant persister-derived CCL20 impairs PD-1 blockade in EGFR-mutant LUAD, preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42549034/
- BRAF fusions define therapeutic vulnerability and tyrosine kinase inhibitor resistance in non-small cell lung cancer. (Lung Cancer). Retrospective genomic series of 97 BRAF-fusion NSCLC including 54 acquired cases at TKI resistance, characterising fusion partners and vulnerabilities. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42546514/
- Prophylactic folinic acid prevents pemetrexed myelosuppression: A randomized trial toward safer treatment with chemotherapy in non-small cell lung cancer. (Neoplasia). Small randomized trial (n=50) showing prophylactic folinic acid increased neutrophil counts after pemetrexed, relevant to osimertinib-pemetrexed tolerability. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42541899/
- Predicting response to immune checkpoint inhibitor plus chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant lung adenocarcinoma following first-generation TKI resistance: a multicenter deep learning study. (Front Immunol). Multicentre CT deep-learning model (n=490, two validation cohorts) predicting ICI-chemotherapy response after first-generation TKI resistance without T790M. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42539527/
- Molecular testing, first-line treatment patterns, and survival in metastatic Colombian non-small cell lung cancer: the RECAPC multicenter registry. (Front Oncol). Colombian multicentre registry describing molecular testing, first-line treatment and survival in metastatic NSCLC, relevant to between-country access differences. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42539508/
- Efficacy and safety of antibody-drug conjugates in EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer after tyrosine kinase inhibitor resistance: a systematic review and meta-analysis. (Front Oncol). Meta-analysis of 9 studies (n=1092) of ADCs after EGFR-TKI resistance, with pooled ORR 44.7% and sac-TMT RCTs giving pooled PFS HR 0.40 and OS HR 0.57. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42534809/
- Inhibition of PGC1β-dependent mitochondrial biogenesis enhances EGFR-targeted therapy in lung cancer. (EMBO Mol Med). EMBO Mol Med preclinical study showing PGC1β-driven mitochondrial biogenesis rebounds at osimertinib resistance and that CPI-613 plus osimertinib suppressed resistant tumours. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42533181/
- Treatment patterns and outcomes of second/third-line therapy in advanced non-small cell lung cancer with actionable genomic alterations (RECAP). (Clin Exp Metastasis). Six-centre Chinese real-world cohort (n=658, 90% EGFR) describing second- and third-line treatment patterns after targeted therapy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42530663/
- CLOCK confers EGFR-TKIs resistance in non-small cell lung cancer by transcriptionally upregulating LAMC2 expression and activating multiple kinase signaling. (Arch Biochem Biophys). Preclinical in vitro/in vivo study proposing CLOCK-LAMC2 signalling as a driver of gefitinib/osimertinib resistance, a mechanistic angle without clinical testing. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42526733/
- MET-Aberrant non-small cell lung cancer: from kinase dependence to cell-surface targetability-mechanistic basis and biomarker framework for bispecific antibodies and antibody-drug conjugates. (Transl Oncol). Review proposing a kinase-dependence/surface-targetability framework for MET-directed TKIs, bispecifics and ADCs, relevant to MET strategies after EGFR TKIs but without new data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42526383/
- Fourth-generation allosteric EGFR inhibitors: Structure-based scaffold analysis and SAR exploration. (Bioorg Chem). Medicinal-chemistry review of 185 fourth-generation allosteric EGFR inhibitors aimed at C797S, relevant to next-generation agents but with no clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42526135/
- AZ'4331 is a novel pan-TEAD auto-palmitoylation inhibitor for the treatment of Hippo pathway-altered cancers. (Mol Cancer Ther). Preclinical characterisation of the pan-TEAD palmitoylation inhibitor AZ'4331, which enhanced osimertinib efficacy in EGFR-mutant NSCLC models, an adaptive-resistance strategy without clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42525965/
- Efficacy and safety of TROP2 antibody-drug conjugate-containing regimen in the patients with HER2 negative metastatic breast cancer and advanced non-small-cell lung cancer. (J Chemother). Real-world cohort of 130 patients (75 NSCLC) on TROP2 ADCs reporting NSCLC median PFS 10.0 months, but retrospective, mixed tumours and no EGFR-specific analysis. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42525843/
- Treatment Patterns and Survival Outcomes in a Real-World Cohort of 263 Patients with EGFR L858R-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Focus on EGFR-TKI Selection. (Asian Pac J Cancer Prev). Single-centre Vietnamese real-world cohort of 263 L858R patients showing better PFS/OS with osimertinib than 1st/2nd-generation TKIs (PFS HR 0.37), a moderately large cohort from a lower-resource setting. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42522829/
- Development of gemcitabine-modified multimodal miR-129 mimic as a novel therapeutic in non-small cell lung cancer. (Mol Ther Nucleic Acids). Preclinical study of a gemcitabine-modified miR-129 mimic active at nanomolar levels in erlotinib- and osimertinib-resistant NSCLC models, early-stage with no clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42519574/
- CDDO-Me Overcomes Gefitinib Resistance in NSCLC by Targeting the Src/STAT3 Axis to Induce Apoptosis and Pyroptosis. (Int J Mol Sci). In vitro study showing bardoxolone methyl (CDDO-Me) resensitises gefitinib-resistant NSCLC cells via Src/STAT3 inhibition, a preclinical resistance strategy with no in vivo or clinical data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42511824/
- The microRNA Expression Signature of Lung Adenocarcinoma Harboring EGFR Mutations: Identification of Therapeutic Targets for EGFR-TKI Combination Therapy. (Int J Mol Sci). miRNA profiling of EGFR-mutant LUAD specimens nominated CDK4 via miR-206 and showed CDK4/6 inhibitor plus osimertinib synergy in vitro, a preclinical combination rationale. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42511755/
- The genomic landscape and treatment outcomes associated with CDKN2A/MTAP loss in patients with non-small cell lung cancer. (Clin Cancer Res). Large real-world genomic dataset (16,947 patients plus 10,996 validation) showing CDKN2A/MTAP deletion is enriched for EGFR and other drivers and linked to shorter time to next treatment on ICI-chemo/chemo. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42507545/
- Cost-effectiveness of first-line osimertinib plus chemotherapy versus amivantamab plus lazertinib for advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC in the US and China. (Ther Adv Med Oncol). US/China cost-effectiveness model comparing FLAURA2 and MARIPOSA regimens, finding neither combination cost-effective in the US, relevant to cross-country access differences. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42502359/
- HDAC6 promotes osimertinib resistance evolution in non-small cell lung cancer by activating EGFR degradation through the ubiquitin-proteasome pathway. (Acta Biochim Biophys Sin (Shanghai)). In vitro osimertinib-resistance evolution model implicating HDAC6-driven EGFR degradation, with HDAC6 inhibition restoring sensitivity, a preclinical mechanism with a druggable angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42502066/
- Development and validation of a risk-stratification model for individualized management of leptomeningeal metastases in EGFR- mutant NSCLC (LM-Index). (Lung Cancer). Multicentre retrospective prognostic score for leptomeningeal metastases in EGFR-mutant NSCLC (derivation n=350, external validation n=302; C-index 0.73), clinically useful but prognostic rather than treatment efficacy data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42501706/
- Cost-effectiveness analysis of first-line gefitinib plus anlotinib treatment for patients with stage IIIB-IV EGFR-mutated NSCLC in China. (Front Public Health). Chinese cost-effectiveness model of first-line gefitinib plus anlotinib (FL-ALTER) finding an ICER of about $196,653/QALY, relevant to access and pricing. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42500005/
- Comprehensive molecular profiling of advanced NSCLC in Greek patients:A prospective HeCOG study. (Cancer Treat Res Commun). Exploratory molecular-profiling analysis of 200 Greek advanced NSCLC patients in the prospective CLIMEDIN trial, finding longer OS in EGFR-mutant disease; the randomisation tested a digital intervention, not treatment. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42497607/
- Drug-specific timing and fatality patterns of EGFR-TKI-associated interstitial lung disease in NSCLC-related JADER reports. (Naunyn Schmiedebergs Arch Pharmacol). Japanese pharmacovigilance analysis of 3206 EGFR-TKI ILD reports finding lower reported fatality with osimertinib (10.8%) than gefitinib, erlotinib or afatinib, a large but spontaneous-report dataset. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42496703/
- Price trend, clinical benefit and within-class competition of drugs for metastatic Non-small cell lung cancer in China: a retrospective cohort study. (Lancet Reg Health West Pac). Lancet Regional Health study of 51 targeted NSCLC drugs in China showing NRDL negotiation, not within-class competition, drove price cuts and that price did not track ESMO-MCBS benefit, relevant to access differences. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42495089/
- Activation of the NF-κB/ALDH1A1 signaling promotes non-mutational resistance to EGFR-TKIs in non-small cell lung cancer. (Oncogene). Oncogene study using cell lines, organoids and in vivo models identifying NF-kappaB/ALDH1A1 feedback as non-mutational EGFR-TKI resistance, reversible with disulfiram or EGCG combinations, preclinical but with a repurposable-drug angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42493544/
- PAX5 modulates vascularization and contributes to neuroendocrine lineage transition during carcinoma progression. (J Clin Invest). JCI mouse-model study showing PAX5 drives an angiogenic microenvironment and neuroendocrine transition, including in EGFR ex19del/T790M adenocarcinomas, reversible with antiangiogenics, mechanistic insight into small-cell transformation. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42490143/
- Safety and Efficacy of Aumolertinib as First-Line Treatment in EGFR-Mutant Lung Adenosquamous Carcinoma: A Multicenter, Single-Arm, Prospective Phase II Study (ARISE Study). (MedComm (2020)). Prospective phase II ARISE trial of first-line aumolertinib in EGFR-mutant adenosquamous carcinoma (median PFS 11.1 months, ORR 58.3%), but terminated early with only 12 patients. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42488708/
- Utilizing Machine Learning to Identify Multimodal Signatures for Patients Who Would Benefit from the Addition of Tremelimumab to Durvalumab and Chemotherapy (TRIDENT). (Clin Cancer Res). Post hoc machine-learning analysis of phase III POSEIDON identifying EGFR wild-type and KRAS/STK11 status among predictors of OS benefit from adding tremelimumab, exploratory and unvalidated. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42485106/
- Pharmacoeconomic Analysis of Osimertinib Compared to Earlier-Generation EGFR Inhibitors in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC: A Systematic Review. (Cancer Med). PRISMA systematic review of nine economic evaluations finding first-line osimertinib ICERs exceed thresholds in the US and Europe, relevant to access differences between jurisdictions. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42484063/
- Development and validation of a nomogram for predicting the prognosis of lung cancer patients with brain metastases harboring EGFR or ALK mutations: a multicenter retrospective study. (Sci Rep). Multicentre retrospective nomogram for OS in 534 EGFR/ALK-positive patients with brain metastases, with an external validation cohort of 314. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42481748/
- Pulsatile High-dose Furmonertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC With Leptomeningeal Metastasis (NCT07348965). Small (n=42) academic study of pulsatile high-dose alternate-day furmonertinib (320 mg) vs 160 mg daily for EGFR-mutant leptomeningeal metastases, a CNS-efficacy question in an unphased design. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07348965
- Efficacy and Safety of SKB264 Plus Anlotinib in EGFR-TKI-Resistant Advanced NSCLC With Liver Metastasis (NCT07545213). Small single-centre phase 2 (n=27) of the TROP2 ADC SKB264 (sacituzumab tirumotecan) plus anlotinib after EGFR-TKI resistance in liver-metastatic NSCLC, compared with historical controls. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07545213
- An EAP Treatment Protocol of Zipalertinib (NCT07601399). Taiho expanded-access protocol for zipalertinib in EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC after platinum with or without amivantamab, signalling access ahead of approval but generating no efficacy data. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07601399
- Osimertinib Combined With Savolitinib in the Treatment of NSCLC With Low Copy Number MET Amplification (NCT07322783). Single-centre phase 2 (n=60) of osimertinib plus savolitinib in osimertinib-resistant NSCLC with low-copy MET amplification, a post-osimertinib MET strategy at modest scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07322783
- Boosting Osimertinib Blood Brain Barrier Penetration (NCT07485452). Tiny (n=7) proof-of-concept phase 2 testing whether the ABCG2 inhibitor febuxostat raises osimertinib CSF penetration, a pharmacokinetic CNS question. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07485452
- JS212 in Combination With JS111 as First-Line Treatment in EGFR-Mutant Advanced NSCLC (NCT07714304). Small single-arm study (n=20) of first-line JS212 plus the TKI JS111 in EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC, an early-stage novel-agent combination. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07714304
- ctDNA Monitoring Guides the Treatment of NSCLC With Befotertinib Combined With Radiotherapy (NCT07410611). Phase 2 (n=84) single-centre study using ctDNA monitoring to guide befotertinib plus radiotherapy in EGFR-mutant oligometastatic NSCLC, a ctDNA-guided strategy at modest scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07410611
- First-line Therapy With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor(EGFR-TKI) Combined With Trastuzumab Rezetecan for Advanced Non-small Cell Lung Cancer(NSCLC) Harboring EGFR Mutations Concomitant With Human Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor 2(HER2) Alterations (NCT07699952). Small academic phase 2 (n=35) of first-line almonertinib plus the HER2 ADC trastuzumab rezetecan for EGFR-mutant NSCLC with HER2 co-alterations or expression, a novel ADC combination at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07699952
- Targeted Induction Therapy for Stage III Unresectable EGFR Mutant Positive NSCLC (NCT07742332). Investigator-led phase 2 (n=160) testing furmonertinib-chemotherapy induction before chemoradiotherapy in unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC, with a sparse registry description. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07742332
- A Study of VRN110755 in Patients With EGFR-Mutant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07699328). First-in-human phase 1/2 (n=315, Voronoi) of the selective oral EGFR inhibitor VRN110755 including TKI-resistant patients, a new-agent registration with no data yet. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07699328
- SYS6010 Versus Docetaxel for Previously Treated EGFR Wild-type NSCLC: Phase Ⅲ (NCT07442565). Industry phase III (n=506, CSPC) of SYS6010 vs docetaxel, but in previously treated EGFR wild-type non-squamous NSCLC, so it falls outside the EGFR-mutant focus. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07442565
- A Phase II Study of Sacituzumab Tirumotecan in Combination With Furmonertinib in Patients With Non-squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Who Have Progressed After EGFR-TKI and Platinum-Based Chemotherapy (NCT07548060). Small single-centre phase 2 (n=25) of sacituzumab tirumotecan plus furmonertinib after progression on EGFR-TKI and platinum, a post-TKI ADC combination. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07548060
- An Open-Label, Single-Arm, Dose-Escalation Phase I Study Evaluating the Safety, Tolerability, and Preliminary Efficacy of EDB-102 in Patients With EGFR L858R-Mutant, Third-Generation TKI-Resistant Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Liver Metastases (NCT07461727). Phase I dose escalation (n=15) of EDB-102, an LNP-delivered CRISPR-Cas9 therapy designed to disrupt EGFR L858R after third-generation TKI resistance, a novel modality at first-in-human stage. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07461727
- Telisotuzumab Vedotin and Osimertinib for the Treatment of Progressive, Incurable, Non Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07323641). Academic phase II (n=60, UCLA Jonsson) of telisotuzumab vedotin (c-Met ADC) plus osimertinib in progressive NSCLC, a MET-directed post-EGFR-TKI strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07323641
- Thiotepa Administration Via Ommaya Reservoir for Leptomeningeal Metastases in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Resistant to EGFR-TKI. (NCT07778940). Phase II (n=50) of intrathecal thiotepa via Ommaya reservoir for leptomeningeal metastases after EGFR-TKI resistance, a CNS strategy at single-centre scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07778940
- Andamertinib With or Without Platinum-doublet Chemotherapy Versus Platinum-doublet Chemotherapy in Patients With NSCLC Harboring Atypical EGFR Mutations (NCT07336732). Randomised industry trial of first-line andamertinib with or without chemotherapy vs chemotherapy in atypical EGFR mutations, designed as phase II/III but registered as phase 2 with 40 patients. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07336732
- Phase II Clinical Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics and Antitumor Activity of ALK202 for Injection in Combination With Drugs in NSCLC Participants. (NCT07603791). Industry phase II (n=252) of ALK202 combined with osimertinib in EGFR-TKI-pretreated and first-line EGFR-mutant cohorts (plus EGFR wild-type cohorts), a new-agent combination with no data yet. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07603791
- Observational Multicenter Study in Patients Receiving Chemotherapy and Amivantamab for Metastatic Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07563205). French GFPC observational study (n=100) of real-world amivantamab-chemotherapy, both first line in exon 20 insertions and after osimertinib in common EGFR mutations, under early access. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07563205
- A Clinical Trial on the Outcomes of Comprehensive Enhanced Prophylaxis Management (CEPM) in Chinese Patients With EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC Receiving Amivantamab-Based Regimens (NCT07469488). Phase 4 study (n=122, Shanghai Pulmonary Hospital) of enhanced dermatologic, infusion-reaction and VTE prophylaxis with amivantamab regimens in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a supportive-care question for a key new regimen. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07469488
- A Study of Neoadjuvant Amivantamab With Either Lazertinib or Chemotherapy in Participants With Resectable EGFR-Mutated NSCLC (NCT07586202). Janssen phase 2 (n=68) of neoadjuvant amivantamab with lazertinib or chemotherapy in resectable stage II-IIIB EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an important perioperative question at phase 2 scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07586202
- Furmonertinib 160mg vs 80mg + Chemotherapy in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC With Brain Metastases: Efficacy and Safety Study (NCT07365410). Phase 2 (n=60) comparing furmonertinib 160 mg vs furmonertinib 80 mg plus chemotherapy first line in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases, a CNS-focused head-to-head at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07365410
- A Clinical Study for the Efficacy and Safety of BL0020 Injection in Patients With Small Cell Lung Cancer Transformed From Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Following EGFR TKI Therapy (NCT07414927). Phase 1/2 (n=48) of BL0020 specifically for small-cell transformation after EGFR-TKI therapy, a novel agent for a transformation-resistance setting with no data yet. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07414927
- A Phase Ⅰ/Ⅱa Clinical Study of GEN-725 in Combination With Dositinib (NCT07362940). Phase I/IIa (n=110) of GEN-725 plus the EGFR-TKI dositinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an early new-agent combination. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07362940
- A Phase II Clinical Trial of Ivonescimab Plus Third-generation EGFR-TKIs in Advanced Non-small Cell Lung Cancer With Gradual or Potential Progression After EGFR-TKIs Treatment (NCT07669051). Small single-centre phase II (n=36) of ivonescimab added to third-generation EGFR-TKI for slow or potential progression, a post-TKI strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07669051
- Phase II Study of Sacituzumab Tirumotecan in Combination With Osimertinib for Neoadjuvant Treatment in Patients With Resectable Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor (EGFR)-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07329322). Kelun-sponsored phase II (n=30) of neoadjuvant sacituzumab tirumotecan plus osimertinib in resectable EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an ADC combination in the perioperative setting. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07329322
- A Prospective Study of Firmonertinib as First-line Adaptive Therapy Guided by Dynamic ctDNA (MRD) Changes in Locally Advanced or Metastatic EGFR-mutated Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07661173). Phase 2 (n=154) of ctDNA/MRD-guided adaptive first-line firmonertinib escalating to chemotherapy with or without bevacizumab in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a ctDNA-guided strategy at single-centre scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07661173
- Study of NXP900 With Osimertinib in Subjects With Advanced, EGFR-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07315113). Phase 1b (n=18, Nuvectis) of the SRC/YES1 inhibitor NXP900 added to osimertinib in progressing EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an early resistance-directed combination. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07315113
- A Randomized Study to Evaluate the Efficacy of a Reduced Starting Dose of Lazertinib (Leclaza) and Preemtive Magnesium Supplementation to Prevent Lazertinib(Leclaza)-Induced Peripheral Neuropathy (NCT07774520). Large randomised phase 2 (n=1173, Samsung Medical Center) testing reduced-dose lazertinib with preemptive magnesium to prevent neuropathy in newly diagnosed EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a tolerability rather than efficacy question. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07774520
- Vebreltinib Plus Furmonertinib in Patients With EGFR-mutated Advanced Non-small Cell Lung Cancer and High PD-L1 Expression (NCT07655622). Small phase Ib/II (n=45) of first-line vebreltinib (MET TKI) plus furmonertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with PD-L1 at least 50%, an exploratory MET combination. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07655622
- Neoadjuvant Sacituzumab Tirumotecan and Limertinib for Potentially Resectable Stage Ⅲ EGFR-mutant Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07323056). Single-arm phase 2 (n=36) of neoadjuvant sacituzumab tirumotecan plus limertinib for potentially resectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an ADC perioperative strategy at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07323056
- Collaborative Clinical-translational Cohort of Amivantamab Plus Lazertinib and Amivantamab Plus Chemotherapy in Patients With EGFR-mutant, Locally Advanced or Metastatic/Recurrent Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (INSTAR Study) (NCT07507188). Translational phase 2 (n=80, Yonsei) of subcutaneous amivantamab with lazertinib first line or with chemotherapy after third-generation TKI, primarily examining immunomodulatory tumour effects. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07507188
- Befotertinib Plus Chemotherapy With an MRD-guided Adaptive Strategy for Treatment Escalation and Response Optimization in EGFR-mutated NSCLC Patients (NCT07709585). Multicentre phase II (n=124) of MRD-guided escalation from befotertinib to befotertinib-chemotherapy in untreated EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a ctDNA-guided strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07709585
- TILs Plus Third-Generation EGFR-TKI Therapy for TKI-Resistant NSCLC (NCT07498595). Early-phase single-arm study (n=50) of tumour-infiltrating lymphocytes plus third-generation EGFR-TKI after TKI resistance, an experimental cell-therapy approach. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07498595
- A Study of SKB571 Combination Therapy in Participants With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC) (NCT07622186). Kelun phase II (n=150) of the new agent SKB571 combined with osimertinib, furmonertinib, chemotherapy or pembrolizumab in NSCLC, an early-stage combination with EGFR cohorts. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07622186
- Firmonertinib in Perioperative Treatment of Resectable Stage II-IIIB EGFR-mutated Lung Adenocarcinoma (NCT07622706). Single-arm phase II (n=60, Peking University) of perioperative firmonertinib at different doses and durations in resectable stage II-IIIB EGFR-mutant LUAD. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07622706
- SYS6090 Combination Therapy in Advanced Lung Cancer (NCT07472647). Large multi-cohort phase Ib/II (n=596, JMT-Bio) of SYS6090 combinations including with the EGFR ADC SYS6010 in advanced lung cancer, an early new-agent programme not restricted to EGFR-mutant disease. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07472647
- The Study Of Andamertinib Plus Anlotinib In Patients With Locally Advanced or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07780578). Single-arm phase 2 (n=34, Fudan) of andamertinib plus anlotinib after third-generation EGFR-TKI, a small post-osimertinib option study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07780578
- A Phase 1 Study of EPI-326 in EGFR-mutant NSCLC and HNSCC (NCT07462377). First-in-human phase 1 (n=110, EpiBiologics) of EPI-326 in EGFR-mutant NSCLC and HNSCC, an early new-agent (likely degrader-type) registration. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07462377
- Osimertinib Combined With Intracranial SRT for EGFR-Mutant NSCLC With Symptomatic Brain Metastases (NCT07491211). Multicentre retrospective real-world study (n=300, Fudan) of first-line osimertinib plus early intracranial SRT for symptomatic brain metastases, a CNS question with a retrospective design. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07491211
- Limertinib Plus Radiotherapy for EGFR-Mutant NSCLC With Brain Metastases (NCT07413952). Single-arm phase II (n=45) of first-line limertinib plus radiotherapy for EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases, a CNS-efficacy study at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07413952
- Ivonescimab Alone And With Carboplatin/Pemetrexed For NSCLC (NCT07405190). MGH phase II (n=46) of ivonescimab alone in TKI- and chemo-pretreated NSCLC with actionable alterations including EGFR, a post-TKI option at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07405190
- A Global Phase II/III Clinical Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of HLX43 Monotherapy or HLX43 in Combination With HLX07 Versus Docetaxel in Advanced or Metastatic Squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07459751). Large global phase II/III (n=706, Henlius) of HLX43 with or without the anti-EGFR antibody HLX07 vs docetaxel in pretreated squamous NSCLC, not an EGFR-mutant population. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07459751
- A Phase II/III Clinical Study to Evaluate HLX07 in Combination With Serplulimab and Chemotherapy Versus Placebo in Combination With Serplulimab or Pembrolizumab and Chemotherapy as First-Line Treatment in Advanced Squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07318883). Phase II/III (n=720, Henlius) adding the anti-EGFR antibody HLX07 to serplulimab-chemotherapy first line in squamous NSCLC, not an EGFR-mutant population. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07318883
- A Phase Ib Study of HS-10504 Combined Therapy in NSCLC (NCT07414953). Hansoh phase Ib (n=400) of HS-10504 combined with several ADCs and other agents in NSCLC, an early new-agent combination platform. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07414953
- A Study to Investigate ANS02 in Participants With EGFR Mutation-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07538804). First-in-human phase I/II (n=294, Avistone) of ANS02 in EGFR mutation-positive NSCLC, a new-agent registration with no data yet. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07538804
- ctDNA-driven Adaptive Proton Craniospinal Irradiation in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Leptomeningeal Metastasis After Resistance to Third-Generation TKIs in the Consolidation Phase (NCT07751536). Study (n=84) using CSF ctDNA clearance to guide proton craniospinal irradiation vs intrathecal pemetrexed consolidation for leptomeningeal metastases after third-generation TKI, a ctDNA-guided CNS strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07751536
- A Phase 2 Study of ABSK043 Combined With Osimertinib (NCT07710612). Industry phase 2 (n=72, Abbisko) of ABSK043 (oral PD-L1 inhibitor) plus osimertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an early combination study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07710612
- An Open-label, Single-arm Study of Prophylaxis for Datopotamab Deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) -Related Stomatitis in Eligible Patients With Metastatic or Inoperable Locally Recurrent Breast Cancer or Locally Advanced or Metastatic Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Mutated Non-small Cell Lung Cancer. (NCT07357597). AstraZeneca single-arm phase 4 (n=100) of dexamethasone mouthwash prophylaxis for Dato-DXd stomatitis including EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a supportive-care study for a new ADC. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07357597
- Treatment for Advanced Non-small Cell Lung Cancer With Actionable Genomic Alterations After Targeted Treatment and Chemotherapy (An Expanded Lung-MAP Treatment Trial) (NCT07393555). SWOG Lung-MAP phase II (n=117) comparing sacituzumab govitecan, ivonescimab or both after targeted therapy and chemotherapy in AGA-positive NSCLC including EGFR, a cooperative-group post-TKI study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07393555
- Local Ablative Therapy to Oligoresidual Metastasis in EGFR Mutated Non-small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07379476). Randomised phase II (n=64, CUHK) of local ablative therapy to oligoresidual disease after 3-6 months of first-line osimertinib vs osimertinib alone, a relevant but modestly sized local-therapy question. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07379476
- A ctDNA-guided Phase II Trial of Osimertinib in Combination With Sacituzumab Tirumotecan in EGFR-mutated Advanced NSCLC Patients With Positive ctDNA After lead-in Osimertinib Monotherapy (NCT07375316). Phase II (n=120) adding sacituzumab tirumotecan to osimertinib for patients still ctDNA-positive after lead-in osimertinib, a ctDNA-guided ADC intensification strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07375316
- Zorifertinib With Osimertinib for NSCLC With Meningeal Progression (NCT07361705). Phase 1 (n=42) of zorifertinib plus osimertinib for leptomeningeal progression on osimertinib, a small CNS-focused combination study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07361705
- Safety and Efficacy of Intrathecal Sacituzumab Tirumotecan for Leptomeningeal Metastasis in EGFR-TKI-Resistant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07702305). Phase I dose-escalation (n=15) of intrathecal sacituzumab tirumotecan via Ommaya for leptomeningeal metastasis after EGFR-TKI, a novel CNS ADC delivery approach at very small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07702305
- Zorifertinib Plus Intra-Ommaya CSF Chemotherapy for Leptomeningeal Progression in NSCLC After Third-Generation EGFR-TKI (NCT07551414). Phase II (n=38) of zorifertinib plus intra-Ommaya chemotherapy for leptomeningeal progression after third-generation EGFR-TKI, a CNS strategy at small scale. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07551414
- PHASE 1b STUDY OF HS-20122 COMBINED THERAPY IN NSCLC (NCT07645222). Hansoh phase 1b (n=396) of HS-20122 combined with aumolertinib or adebrelimab-based regimens in NSCLC, an early new-agent combination with an EGFR-TKI arm. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07645222
- Sacituzumab Tirumotecan（Sac-TMT） and Brain Radotherapy for EGFR+ NSCLC With Brain Mets (NCT07789444). Phase II (n=53) of sacituzumab tirumotecan plus brain radiotherapy for intracranial progression after third-generation EGFR-TKI, a CNS-focused post-TKI ADC study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07789444
- Phase II Trial of Amivantamab Plus Monochemotherapy in Platinum Unfit NSCLC Patients With EGFR exon20 Insertion Mutations. (NCT07392658). Small phase II (n=33) of amivantamab plus monochemotherapy in platinum-unfit exon 20 insertion NSCLC, addressing a frail subgroup. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07392658
- JS212 Plus JS111 in EGFR-Mutant Advanced NSCLC: A Phase II Study (NCT07518160). Junshi phase II (n=35) of JS212 plus JS111 after EGFR-TKI progression, an early new-agent combination aiming at a phase III dose. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07518160
- Hippocampus-Protective Radiotherapy Combined With Osimertinib for Symptomatic Brain Metastases in EGFR-Mutated Lung Cancer (NCT07505173). Single-arm phase II (n=74) of hippocampal-sparing WBRT with boost plus osimertinib for symptomatic brain metastases with an intracranial PFS endpoint, a CNS strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07505173
- A Phase l Clinical Study to Evalute the Safety,Tolerability,Pharmacokinetic Characteristics,and Preliminary Anti-tumor Efficacy of HJ-004-02 Tablets in Patients With Non-squamous Non-small Cell Lung Cancer With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor (EGFR) Mutations (NCT07361237). First-in-human phase I (n=36, Tongji University) of the oral agent HJ-004-02 in EGFR-mutant non-squamous NSCLC, an early new-agent registration. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07361237
- SYS6010 Combined With Enlonstobart Versus Immunotherapy+ Platinum-based Chemotherapy for Patients With PD-L1-Positive Locally Advanced or Metastatic NSCLC（SYNSTAR 04） (NCT07633873). Phase III (SYNSTAR 04, n=500, CSPC) of the EGFR-directed ADC SYS6010 plus enlonstobart vs immunotherapy-chemotherapy first line in PD-L1-positive NSCLC, not an EGFR-mutant population. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07633873
- Global, Multicenter Study of AMT-116 Versus Investigator's Choice in Participants With Advanced or Metastatic Non-squamous EGFR-Wildtype Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC) With Progression on or After Platinum-Based Chemotherapy and Anti-PD-1/PD-L1 Immunotherapy (NCT07718737). Global phase 2/3 (n=512, Multitude) of AMT-116 vs docetaxel-based therapy, but in EGFR wild-type non-squamous NSCLC, outside the EGFR-mutant focus. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07718737
- A Non-interventional, Real-world Study on the Adjuvant Treatment of Chinese EGFR-positive Stage IA NSCLC Patients With High-risk Factors Using Third-generation EGFR-TKIs. (NCT07686185). Real-world cohort (n=500) of adjuvant third-generation EGFR-TKIs in high-risk stage IA EGFR-mutant NSCLC, a population outside ADAURA's eligibility, but non-randomised. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07686185
- Sacituzumab Tirumotecan Plus Third-Generation TKI With/Without Radiotherapy for EGFR-Mutant NSCLC Brain Metastases (NCT07343479). Single-arm phase II (n=45) of sacituzumab tirumotecan plus third-generation TKI with or without radiotherapy for EGFR-mutant brain metastases, a CNS ADC strategy with a minimal registry description. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07343479
- A Study of Ivonescimab in Combination With Dato-DXd or Osimertinib in People With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07535437). MSKCC phase 1/2 (n=66) of ivonescimab combined with Dato-DXd or osimertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, an early novel-combination study. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07535437
- A Phase II Study of AK146D1 in Combination With AK112 in Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NCT07669779). Akeso phase II (n=348) of the new agent AK146D1 with ivonescimab, chemotherapy or osimertinib in advanced NSCLC, an early combination study with EGFR-relevant arms. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07669779
- A Real World Study of Participants With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC) With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Mutation (EGFRm) (NCT07680907). Janssen real-world observational study (n=250) of treatments and outcomes in advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC, likely in Brazil, relevant to access and outcomes outside trial settings. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07680907
- Double-Dose Third-Generation EGFR-TKI Plus Bevacizumab and Intrathecal Chemotherapy for Refractory Leptomeningeal Metastatic NSCLC: A Phase II Study (NCT07398599). Small study (n=30) of double-dose third-generation EGFR-TKI with bevacizumab and intrathecal chemotherapy for refractory leptomeningeal metastases, a CNS salvage strategy. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07398599
- Phase 2 Study of Poziotinib in Participants With NSCLC Having EGFR or HER2 Exon 20 Insertion Mutation (NCT03318939).
