Analysis of >13,000 NSCLC samples finding MTAP loss in 17-24% of EGFR-mutant tumours without impact on osimertinib outcomes, plus preclinical PRMT5 inhibitor activity including an osimertinib-resistant PDX. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41833902/
- Respiratory viral infections prime accelerated lung cancer growth. (Cell). Cell study linking severe COVID-19 hospitalisation to later lung cancer risk and showing in mice that viral pneumonia accelerates tumour growth via neutrophil-driven immunosuppression. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41819102/
- Ageing promotes metastasis via activation of the integrated stress response. (Nature). Nature mouse study showing ageing promotes KRAS-driven LUAD metastasis through ISR-ATF4 activation with a targetable glutamine dependency; preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41813904/
- Vascular STING activation facilitates NK cell anti-tumor immunity in small cell lung cancer. (Cancer Cell). Cancer Cell study showing microvasculature excludes NK cells from MHC-I-low SCLC and vascular STING activation restores NK infiltration and killing; preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41791380/
- Cardiac radiosensitivity in the era of thoracic chemoradiotherapy and immunotherapy: a scoping review. (Lancet Oncol). Lancet Oncology scoping review of cardiac toxicity with chemoradiotherapy plus ICI consolidation in stage III NSCLC, based on ten clinical studies. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41785903/
- Medical thoracoscopy with talc poudrage and indwelling pleural catheter insertion versus medical thoracoscopy with talc poudrage alone for patients with symptomatic malignant pleural effusion (TACTIC): a randomised, controlled phase 3 trial. (Lancet Respir Med). UK phase 3 TACTIC RCT of adding an indwelling pleural catheter to thoracoscopic talc poudrage for malignant pleural effusion of mixed primary cancers; not lung-cancer specific. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41763237/
- PROSPECT-LUNG: A National Clinical Trials Network Trial Advancing Pragmatic Innovation in Cancer Clinical Trials. (J Thorac Oncol). Description of the pragmatic NCTN phase III PROSPECT-Lung design comparing perioperative vs adjuvant immunotherapy strategies in resectable NSCLC; no outcome data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41747891/
- Systemic and Intracranial Efficacy and Safety of Trastuzumab Deruxtecan in Patients With HER2-Mutant NSCLC Across Treatment Lines: Evidence From the TRACER/HERTras Real-World Cohort. (J Thorac Oncol). Multinational real-world cohort of 168 HER2-mutant NSCLC patients on trastuzumab deruxtecan with ORR 54.8%, median PFS 7.2 and OS 18.3 months. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41747890/
- Tumor-Brain Communication Mediates Immune Escape of Lung Cancer. (Cancer Discov). Cancer Discovery article on tumour-brain communication mediating immune escape in lung cancer; no abstract to assess clinical angle. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41746266/
- Identifying Patients With High-Risk Stage I NSCLC Using a Tumor-Informed Plasma ctDNA Assay. (J Thorac Oncol). Prospective cohort of 153 clinical stage I NSCLC patients where preoperative tumour-informed ctDNA (detected in 22.4%) predicted worse RFS (HR 3.81). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41730473/
- Clinical Value of Combining Molecular and Immune Blood Tests to Safely Reduce False Positives in Low-Dose Computed Tomography Lung Cancer Screening. (J Thorac Oncol). BioMILD screening substudy of 304 heavy smokers with suspicious nodules combining a plasma microRNA test and immune signature classifier to reduce LDCT false positives. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41707946/
- TTF-1 Expression in Lung Adenocarcinoma: Clinicopathologic, Genomic, and Immunophenotypic Correlates and Outcomes to Immunotherapy-Based Treatments and KRASG12C Inhibitors. (J Thorac Oncol). Multicohort analysis of 3,297 LUAD patients linking TTF-1 negativity to STK11/KEAP1 co-mutations and worse outcomes with immunotherapy and KRAS G12C inhibitors. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41690367/
- Medicaid Expansion and Stage at Diagnosis, Timely Initiation and Receipt of Guideline-Concordant Treatment, and Survival Among People With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer. (J Clin Oncol). NCDB difference-in-differences study of Medicaid expansion and NSCLC stage, guideline-concordant treatment and 5-year survival in adults 18-64. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41687045/
- Ancestry-Associated Performance Variability of Open-Source AI Models for EGFR Prediction in Lung Cancer. (JAMA Oncol). Cohort study showing open-source AI pathology models for EGFR mutation prediction vary in performance across ancestry groups and cohorts. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41678173/
- Bispecific T-cell Engager (CD3 × EGFR)-Based Triplet Therapy Unlocks CD40/CD40L Crosstalk to Revert Immunosuppression in Third-Generation EGFR-Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor-Refractory NSCLC. (J Thorac Oncol). Preclinical study of a CD3xEGFR T-cell engager plus HDAC inhibitor and 4-1BB agonist reversing immunosuppression in third-generation EGFR-TKI-refractory NSCLC models. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41672197/
- Abrogation of Oncogenic RAS Signaling by a RAS(ON) Inhibitor Doublet Primes Immune-Refractory KRASG12C-Mutant NSCLC for Immune Checkpoint Blockade. (Cancer Discov). Preclinical Cancer Discovery study showing elironrasib plus daraxonrasib RAS(ON) doublet causes durable regressions and sensitises KRAS G12C NSCLC models to checkpoint blockade. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41670434/
- A Game-Changing 20 Years: Progress and Future Directions in Lung Cancer Screening. (J Thorac Oncol). JTO review of 20 years of LDCT lung cancer screening, programmes in 13 countries and AI integration. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41651571/
- Tumour-brain crosstalk restrains cancer immunity via a sensory-sympathetic axis. (Nature). Nature mouse study showing lung adenocarcinoma engages vagal sensory neurons to drive sympathetic beta2-adrenergic immunosuppression; preclinical mechanism. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41639447/
- Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Without Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, 2026.3.0. (J Clin Oncol). ASCO living guideline version 2026.3.0 for stage IV NSCLC without drivers, already superseded by version 2026.3.1 in this same run. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41632927/
- Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, 2026.3.0. (J Clin Oncol). ASCO living guideline version 2026.3.0 for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations (13 new studies), already superseded by version 2026.3.1 in this same run. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41632926/
- Impact of KRAS Mutations and Co-Alterations on Outcomes in Stage III Nonsquamous NSCLC Treated With Chemoradiation and Immunotherapy. (J Thorac Oncol). Multicentre retrospective series of 208 stage III nonsquamous NSCLC patients where KRAS mutation predicted shorter PFS after chemoradiation-durvalumab (16 vs 28 months). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41619904/
- Emerging landscape of KRAS inhibitors in cancer treatment. (Cancer Cell). Cancer Cell review of KRAS G12C and next-generation RAS inhibitors, resistance mechanisms and preliminary clinical efficacy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41616774/
- Armored macrophage-targeted CAR-T cells reset and reprogram the tumor microenvironment and control metastatic cancer growth. (Cancer Cell). Cancer Cell preclinical study of IL-12-armoured FOLR2/TREM2 anti-macrophage CAR-T cells improving survival in metastatic lung and ovarian cancer models. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41576929/
- Cost-Effectiveness of Adjuvant Immunotherapy in Cancer Treatments: A Systematic Review. (JAMA Oncol). Systematic review of economic evaluations of adjuvant immunotherapy across cancers including lung, relevant to access and value. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41569596/
- Critical role for a high-plasticity cell state in lung cancer. (Nature). Nature mouse lineage-tracing study showing ablation of a high-plasticity cell state blocks lung tumour progression; preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41565826/
- Preclinical Characterization and Clinical Activity of RNK08954, a Highly Selective and Orally Bioavailable KRASG12D Inhibitor. (Cancer Discov). Phase Ia of oral KRAS G12D inhibitor RNK08954 (36 evaluable, 4.9 months follow-up) reporting unconfirmed ORR 58.3% in NSCLC at 1,000-1,200 mg; very early data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41557983/
- European cancer mortality predictions for the year 2026: the levelling of female lung cancer mortality. (Ann Oncol). Annals of Oncology projection of 2026 EU cancer mortality highlighting levelling of female lung cancer death rates. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41554652/
- Biology and Clinical Management of Non-V600 BRAF Alterations in NSCLC. (J Thorac Oncol). JTO review of class II/III non-V600 BRAF alterations in NSCLC, including as acquired resistance in oncogene-driven tumours. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41553307/
- Loss of Payload Sensitivity and Other Mechanisms of Resistance to T-DXd in HER2-Mutant NSCLC: Implications for Subsequent Responsiveness to HER2 TKIs. (J Thorac Oncol). Preclinical and clinical analysis of acquired T-DXd resistance in HER2-mutant NSCLC (payload resistance, HER2 domain IV mutations) and implications for subsequent HER2 TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41548807/
- Lymph Node Dissection Guideline Adherence and Survival in Patients With T1N0M0 Lung Adenocarcinoma. (JAMA Oncol). Korean multicentre cohort of T1N0M0 lung adenocarcinoma linking guideline-adherent lymph node dissection with survival by histologic pattern. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41538156/
- Changes in Cardiac Function in Patients Receiving Radiation Therapy for Lung Cancer. (J Thorac Oncol). Prospective multicentre cohort of 125 NSCLC patients showing modest, largely reversible echocardiographic declines in cardiac function after thoracic radiotherapy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41534787/
- Circulating Tumor Cells Predict Response to the DLL3-Targeting Bispecific Antibody Tarlatamab. (Cancer Discov). Prospective cohort of 20 SCLC patients in which DLL3 expression on circulating tumour cells predicted tarlatamab benefit (85% sensitivity, 100% specificity). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41532856/
- PD-1 Blockade-Induced DKK1 Expression by CD8+ T Cells Promotes Blood-Brain Barrier Permeabilization. (Cancer Discov). Cancer Discovery study showing anti-PD-1-induced DKK1 from CD8+ T cells permeabilises the blood-brain barrier, with correlative lung cancer patient MRI and plasma data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41525686/
- Advances in the Basic Sciences in Thoracic Oncology in the Last 20 Years and Their Translational Impact. (J Thorac Oncol). JTO review of 20 years of basic-science advances in thoracic oncology and their translational impact. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41519536/
- AI-enabled virtual spatial proteomics from histopathology for interpretable biomarker discovery in lung cancer. (Nat Med). Nat Med AI model (HEX) generating virtual spatial proteomics from H&E, improving immunotherapy response prediction by 24-39% across six NSCLC cohorts (n=2,298), but it is a retrospective biomarker tool without prospective clinical use. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41491099/
- Journal of Thoracic Oncology 20th Anniversary Series Surgery. (J Thorac Oncol). JTO 20th-anniversary narrative review of two decades of thoracic surgery (TNM 9th edition, sublobar resection, minimally invasive surgery); top-journal floor but no new data. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41483000/
- Generative AI-based low-dose digital subtraction angiography for intra-operative radiation dose reduction: a randomized controlled trial. (Nat Med). Nat Med RCT (n=1,068, 417 with lung cancer) showing generative-AI low-dose DSA cut intra-operative radiation by about two thirds; procedural imaging, not lung cancer treatment. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41482562/
- PET/CT-guided management of immune checkpoint blockade and multi-modal profiling following treatment in long-term responders with metastatic lung cancer in the National Network Genomic Medicine Lung Cancer Germany (nNGM). (Ann Oncol). Ann Oncol retrospective nNGM cohort (n=455) in which PET/CT-guided stopping of immunotherapy after 2+ years of disease control was associated with longer OS (HR 0.35), but treatment groups were not randomised and are subject to selection bias. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41478526/
- Physical Activity During a Course of Radiotherapy for Lung Cancer - Results of the APART-LUNG Study. (Anticancer Res). APART-LUNG pilot (n=19) tracking smartphone step counts during lung radiotherapy, which found no significant change from week 1 to week 5; small feasibility study. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42674679/
- Validity and Feasibility of the Constant-Load Cycle Endurance Test in Lung Cancer Patients Undergoing Radiation Therapy. (Thorac Cancer). Small study (n=33) validating a cycle endurance test that responded to a 4-week pulmonary rehabilitation programme during lung radiotherapy, with no adverse events. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42644403/
- Longitudinal Trajectories and Predictors of Supportive Care Needs After Lung Cancer Surgery: A Prospective Cohort Study. (J Clin Nurs). Prospective cohort (n=287) of supportive-care-need trajectories over the year after lung cancer surgery, with lack of regular exercise linked to persistently high needs; single centre. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42633593/
- Longitudinal computed tomography body composition changes in patients receiving curative radiotherapy for non-small cell lung cancer. (Phys Imaging Radiat Oncol). Retrospective CT body-composition study (n=231, 2,708 scans) in which skeletal muscle loss faster than 0.4%/month after curative radiotherapy predicted worse OS (HR 4.41); single centre. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42633359/
- A Perioperative Multidisciplinary Management Protocol for Patients Undergoing Lung Cancer Resection: Implementation and Outcomes Analysis. (J Vis Exp). Multidisciplinary perioperative bundle (prehabilitation, nutrition, psychological support, early mobilisation) associated with fewer complications after resection, but a non-randomised protocol report. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42611789/
- Malnutrition Severity, Morphofunctional Assessment, and Survival in Patients with Lung Cancer: A Multicentre Prospective Cohort Study. (Lung). Multicentre prospective cohort (n=374) linking GLIM-defined severe malnutrition and morphofunctional measures (handgrip, muscle ultrasound) to one-year mortality before lung cancer treatment; observational, not an intervention trial. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42595921/
- Early On-Treatment Host Fitness Dynamics Refine Prognostic Stratification in Advanced NSCLC Receiving First-Line Immunotherapy. (Cancer Res Treat). Secondary analysis of the prospective multicentre OPTIMUS cohort (n=497) in which a low early on-treatment prognostic nutritional index predicted loss of response to pembrolizumab; nutritional marker only, no intervention. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42594970/
- Long-term suicide risk in lung cancer patients: a nationwide cohort study. (Epidemiol Psychiatr Sci). Nationwide cohort (n=37,215 resected patients) showing suicide risk peaking in the first year after lung cancer surgery (sHR 1.88), relevant to psychosocial care, but observational. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42578364/
- Exercise Modulates Adiposity-induced Immune Dysfunction in NSCLC and Non-tumor-Bearing Lungs: Implications for Lung Cancer Therapy and Interception. (Ann Surg). Mouse study showing voluntary exercise reversed obesity-driven immune suppression in Lewis lung tumours, with supporting bronchoalveolar lavage data from 73 patients; mainly preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42557599/
- Nicotinic Acid Restriction Enhances the Therapeutic Benefit of NAMPT Inhibition in Small-Cell Lung Cancer Models. (Cancer Sci). Preclinical SCLC CDX/PDX study in which dietary nicotinic acid restriction plus NAMPT inhibition outperformed cisplatin-etoposide in most models; mouse data only. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42521252/
- Tobacco Use, Stigma, and Coping in Lung Cancer: A Systematic Review of Their Psychosocial Interactions and Clinical Implications. (Curr Oncol). PRISMA systematic review of how tobacco use, stigma and coping interact to shape psychological outcomes in lung cancer; synthesises mainly observational studies. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42505210/
- Acute and lifelong exercise modulate the tumorigenic potential of human lung cancer cells and their susceptibility to cisplatin. (Exp Physiol). Ex vivo study showing serum from acute and lifelong exercisers altered NSCLC cell growth and cisplatin sensitivity; mechanistic exercise data without patients. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42501047/
- Effect of high-intensity interval training on clinical outcomes in lung cancer patients undergoing surgery: a meta-analysis based on randomized controlled trials. (Front Med (Lausanne)). Meta-analysis of 11 RCTs finding perioperative HIIT reduced pulmonary infection (OR 0.38) and atelectasis (OR 0.30) and improved VO2peak after lung cancer surgery; the included trials are small and partly from Chinese databases. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42500527/
- A retrospective study on the rehabilitation effects of IMB nursing intervention for lung cancer patients after cryoablation. (Discov Oncol). Retrospective non-randomised comparison (n=552) of an IMB-based nursing programme versus routine care after cryoablation, reporting self-efficacy, QoL and complications. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42469552/
- Dietary intervention through bacterial-derived butyrate elicits anti-tumor activity and increases anti-PD-1 response. (Gut Microbes). Mouse study in which dietary inulin and butyrate enhanced anti-PD-1 efficacy through CD8 T cells, with descriptive fibre-intake and microbiome data from 117 NSCLC patients; mainly preclinical. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42454784/
- Effects of prehabilitation on outcomes among patients undergoing surgery for lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials. (J Thorac Dis). Meta-analysis of 15 RCTs (n=1,129) in which prehabilitation improved preoperative 6MWD by about 38 m and reduced pulmonary complications (OR 0.39) and hospital stay after lung cancer surgery. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42444881/
- Effect of Structured Perioperative Education on Postoperative Pain Following Lung Cancer Surgery: A Prospective Non-randomized Interventional Study With 12-Month Follow-Up. (Cureus). Prospective non-randomised study (n=160) of structured perioperative education reporting pain outcomes over 12 months after lung cancer surgery. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42422662/
- Global cancer statistics 2024: GLOBOCAN estimates of incidence and mortality worldwide for 34 cancers in 186 countries. (CA Cancer J Clin). GLOBOCAN 2024 statistics from CA Cancer J Clin, with lung cancer the leading cancer (2.6 million cases, 1.9 million deaths); key burden reference but outside the non-drug intervention scope. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42417444/
- A Prospective Observational Study of Anamorelin During Chemoimmunotherapy in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Cachexia (SPIRAL-ANA). (J Cachexia Sarcopenia Muscle). Prospective multicentre single-arm study (n=123) of anamorelin during chemoimmunotherapy in cachectic NSCLC (ORR 57.9%), relevant to cachexia management but drug-based and compared with a historical control. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42411631/
- BCAA metabolism promotes lung cancer tumorigenesis by enhancing cholesterol biosynthesis. (Cell Rep). Cell Rep study combining nested case-control metabolomics with Kras mouse data showing that a high-BCAA (leucine) diet promotes lung tumorigenesis via cholesterol synthesis and EGFR activation, blocked by atorvastatin; preclinical/risk-focused. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42397743/
- Impact of exercise rehabilitation and behavioral interaction nursing on postoperative quality of life and psychological outcomes in lung cancer patients. (Front Oncol). Retrospective propensity-matched cohort (n=205) associating combined exercise rehabilitation and behavioural nursing with better postoperative QoL and psychological outcomes; non-randomised. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42388625/
- Effectiveness of digital personalized nursing pathway on postoperative rehabilitation in patients with non-small cell lung cancer. (Front Med (Lausanne)). Quasi-experimental study (n=184) in which a digital personalised nursing and rehabilitation pathway lowered postoperative pulmonary complications (10.3% vs 24.7%) and improved FEV1 recovery after NSCLC surgery; not randomised. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42388508/
- The application of collaborative nursing model improve the postoperative pulmonary rehabilitation in patients with lung carcinoma: a retrospective study of 345 cases. (Front Med (Lausanne)). Retrospective comparison (n=345) associating a collaborative nursing model with better pulmonary function recovery and QoL after resection; non-randomised and unbalanced groups. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42388477/
- Synergy of a Low-methionine Diet and Reduced-dose Cisplatinum Eradicates Experimental Lung-cancer Bone Metastases in a Nude-mouse Model. (In Vivo). Nude-mouse study in which a low-methionine diet plus half-dose cisplatin essentially eradicated A549 bone metastases; preclinical dietary intervention only. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42379791/
- Associations between body composition and radiotherapy-related side-effects and health-related quality of life in patients with prostate or lung cancer: sub-analysis of the REQUITE trial. (Radiother Oncol). REQUITE prospective multicentre sub-analysis relating CT body composition to radiotherapy side-effects and HRQoL in lung and prostate cancer. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42364819/
- The effectiveness of non-pharmacological interventions on sleep quality in lung cancer patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (2015-2025). (Support Care Cancer). Meta-analysis of 24 studies (n=2,604) of non-pharmacological interventions for sleep quality in lung cancer, with low-to-moderate evidence quality. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42362784/
- Integrating Exercise and Education into Lung Cancer Care: Results from the OVER-CRF Pilot Study on Cancer-Related Fatigue and Quality of Life. (Curr Oncol). OVER-CRF pilot RCT (n=31) of early vs delayed pulmonary rehabilitation with education during NSCLC treatment, showing high adherence and no adverse events; feasibility-sized. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42346213/
- Wearable technologies for perioperative recovery monitoring in lung cancer surgery: a systematic review of feasibility, recovery outcomes, and evidence certainty. (Front Oncol). PROSPERO-registered systematic review of wearables for perioperative recovery monitoring in lung cancer surgery, with narrative synthesis of only 8 reports. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42318462/
- Efficacy of traditional Chinese exercise on postoperative rehabilitation in lung cancer patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis. (J Thorac Dis). Meta-analysis of 25 RCTs (n=1,834) finding traditional Chinese exercise improved lung function, 6MWD, QoL and anxiety after lung cancer surgery, though with high heterogeneity among small trials. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42306719/
- Exercise interventions for depressive symptoms in adults with lung and digestive cancer: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials. (Front Psychiatry). Meta-analysis of RCTs on exercise for depressive symptoms in lung and digestive cancer patients, relevant to psychosocial non-drug interventions but pooling tumour types. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42305843/
- Mediterranean diet adherence and dietary fat intake in relation to lung cancer outcomes: a prospective cohort study. (Food Funct). UK Biobank cohort (n=191,139) linking Mediterranean diet adherence to lower lung cancer incidence (HR 0.66) and mortality, largely a prevention-epidemiology question in people without cancer at baseline. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42299984/
- Feasibility and acceptability of ExerciseGuideUK for those living with and beyond lung cancer: a mixed methods study. (Support Care Cancer). Mixed-methods feasibility study (n=18) of the web-based ExerciseGuideUK platform for people living with lung cancer, reporting recruitment 30.5% and retention 77%. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42277446/
- Effectiveness of a swallowing rehabilitation program for elderly lung cancer patients after lobectomy: A quasi-experimental study using a non-equivalent control group pretest-posttest design. (J Korean Gerontol Nurs). Quasi-experimental non-randomised study (n=63) finding a postoperative swallowing rehabilitation programme reduced dysphagia in elderly lobectomy patients. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42209257/
- Pharmacological, psychosocial, and brain stimulation interventions in oncologic populations with mental disorders: An umbrella review. (Psychiatry Res). Umbrella review of 82 meta-analyses of interventions for mental disorders in cancer, including mindfulness for anxiety in lung cancer, though nearly all estimates were very low GRADE certainty. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42184575/
- Effectiveness of electrical stimulation for postoperative rehabilitation of lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis. (J Thorac Dis). Meta-analysis of 23 RCTs (n=2,232) finding electrical stimulation after lung cancer surgery improved 6MWD by 33.8 m and shortened stay by 3.2 days, but pain reduction was below the clinically important difference. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42182721/
- Applying digital technologies for remote care in the real life context: A 3-year experimentation with postoperative lung cancer patients. (Medicine (Baltimore)). Retrospective matched comparison (250 vs 250) of the Dignio remote digital rehabilitation platform after lung cancer surgery, reporting better early FEV1/FVC recovery; non-randomised design. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42175423/
- International Consensus on Severe Lung Cancer-The Second Edition. (Transl Lung Cancer Res). Second-edition international consensus (11 recommendations) on managing severe lung cancer in PS 2-4 patients, including supportive care, but not focused on non-drug interventions. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42170297/
- Comparative Efficacy of Non-Pharmacological Interventions on Fatigue, Depression, and Quality of Life in Patients With Lung Cancer: A Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials. (Worldviews Evid Based Nurs). Network meta-analysis of 58 RCTs on 13 non-pharmacological interventions in lung cancer, ranking acupuncture and ACT highest for fatigue and ACT for depression and QoL. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42163071/
- Application of artificial intelligence-assisted education for postoperative pulmonary rehabilitation: a hybrid model of AI and nurse-driven support. (J Cardiothorac Surg). Non-randomised two-period comparison (n=128) in which AI-assisted rehabilitation education after thoracoscopic lung cancer surgery improved exercise adherence and self-efficacy and was associated with fewer complications (4.8% vs 15.4%). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42152114/
- Consensus recommendations on surveillance and survivorship for patients with unresectable/stage IV non-small cell lung cancer after completing Immunotherapy: a Delphi study by Canadian experts. (Lung Cancer). Canadian Delphi consensus (10 experts) on surveillance and survivorship care after completing immunotherapy for stage IV NSCLC, relevant to survivorship but not testing a non-drug intervention. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42127534/
- Insights from a randomized controlled trial on a digital lifestyle intervention in non-small cell lung cancer survivors following inpatient rehabilitation. (Lung Cancer). Multicentre RCT of a 12-week app-based lifestyle intervention after inpatient rehabilitation in NSCLC survivors, but only 20 of 66 planned participants were enrolled and HRQoL did not differ. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42127533/
- CT-based body composition and its change through time in relation to outcomes in participants screened for lung cancer. (EBioMedicine). Retrospective analysis of repeated CTs in 6,187 NELSON screening participants linking muscle and subcutaneous fat loss to lung cancer incidence and mortality, though in a screened rather than cancer population. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42066437/
- Effects of ACSM guideline-based exercise on patients with lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis. (Front Physiol). Meta-analysis of 32 exercise RCTs in lung cancer finding improved QoL (SMD 0.44), larger with high ACSM-guideline adherence (SMD 0.75 vs 0.20). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42064554/
- Brief Report: A Pilot Study Evaluating the Feasibility, Safety, and Compliance With a Fasting-Mimicking Diet in Patients With Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer. (Clin Lung Cancer). Single-arm pilot (n=10) showing one 4-day fasting-mimicking diet was feasible (90% completion) and safe without grade 3-4 events in metastatic NSCLC, mostly on targeted therapy. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42060951/
- Cancer Cachexia Predicts Benefit of Immunotherapy Plus Chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant NSCLC After TKI Resistance. (Anticancer Res). Multicentre retrospective cohort (n=439) of EGFR-mutant NSCLC after TKI failure in which cachexia predicted benefit of ICI-chemotherapy over chemotherapy (OS 13.8 vs 11.2 months). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42049337/
- Clinical efficacy of comprehensive traditional Chinese medicine in adjuvant therapy for stage Ib-IIIa resected non-small cell lung cancer: a multi-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial. (Front Pharmacol). Multicentre placebo-controlled RCT (n=286) of TCM granules plus Huachansu with adjuvant chemotherapy in resected NSCLC showing a borderline DFS benefit (HR 0.73, p=0.045; 2-year DFS difference not significant), but TCM is herbal medicine rather than a non-drug intervention. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42038290/
- Patient-reported outcomes from the LAURA study: osimertinib in patients with unresectable stage III EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer after definitive chemoradiotherapy. (Eur J Cancer). Patient-reported outcomes from the phase 3 LAURA RCT showing QoL was maintained on osimertinib vs placebo after chemoradiotherapy in stage III EGFR-mutated NSCLC; important EGFR trial data but a drug trial, so outside the non-pharmacological criteria it matched. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42019226/
- Associations between early loss of skeletal muscle and osimertinib trough concentrations in patients with advanced EGFR-mutated NSCLC. (Eur J Cancer). START-TKI analysis (n=53) of advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC on osimertinib in which early skeletal muscle loss did not affect drug exposure or PFS but independently predicted shorter OS (aHR 2.04). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41997038/
- Quality of life after preoperative home-based exercise training in patients with lung cancer: a secondary analysis. (J Thorac Dis). Secondary analysis (n=32) of pooled feasibility and RCT data on preoperative home-based exercise, finding 56% were QoL responders and COPD predicted response (OR 13.4). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41988321/
- Prevalence and survival implications of CT-defined low skeletal muscle mass in lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis. (Front Oncol). Meta-analysis of 29 observational studies estimating 40% prevalence of CT-defined low skeletal muscle mass in lung cancer and linking it to shorter OS. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41939462/
- Gut microbiota-associated nutritional-immune status predicts prognosis in postoperative NSCLC patients. (Gut Microbes). Retrospective (n=372) and prospective (n=139) NSCLC cohorts plus mouse FMT experiments linking a high prognostic nutritional index to SCFA-producing gut taxa and better 5-year survival (HR 0.39). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41933285/
- Role of cachexia in advanced non-small cell lung cancer patients treated with EGFR-TKIS. (BMC Cancer). Retrospective cohort (n=247) of EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC on EGFR-TKIs in which baseline cachexia (37%) predicted haematologic toxicity (aOR 2.26) and worse OS (aHR 1.65). https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41928147/
- The heterogeneous nutritional status trajectory and its predictors in elderly patients undergoing lung cancer surgery: a prospective cohort study. (Front Nutr). Prospective single-centre cohort (n=474) of elderly lung cancer surgery patients identifying malnutrition trajectories linked to lower 6-month QoL; descriptive, no intervention. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41923908/
- Meeting highlights from the CHMP 23-26 March 2026 (web). CHMP March 2026 meeting highlights page; its lung-relevant decision (tarlatamab/Imdylltra positive opinion for ES-SCLC) is already recorded separately, so the page adds little beyond it. https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/news/meeting-highlights-committee-medicinal-products-human-use-chmp-23-26-march-2026
